    hep-ph

    A High-Energy Neutrino Collider within a Muon Collider

    Authors: Innes Bigaran, André de Gouvêa, Tao Han, Ian Low, Yang Ma, Zahra Tabrizi, Keping Xie

    Abstract: …vector bosons when their masses are kinematically accessible, while their off-shell effects are captured by the corresponding effective operators. In particular, we show that supplementing the dominant charged-lepton collisions with neutrino-initiated collisions significantly improves the ability to resolve degeneracies among different effective operators,… ▽ More A multi-TeV muon collider is simultaneously a collider for high-energy neutrinos. The radiation of soft $W^\pm$ gauge bosons from the muon beams gives rise to collisions of $μ^+ν$, $μ^-\barν$ and $ν\barν$ at center-of-mass energies close to that of the $μ^+μ^-$ collisions in a controlled and theoretically predictable setting. We focus on the leading partonic processes $μ^+μ^- \to \ell^+\ell'^-$, $ν_μμ^+\to ν_\ell \ell'^+$, $\barν_μμ^- \to \barν_{\ell} \ell'^-$ and $ν_μ\bar ν_μ\to \ell^+\ell'^-$ ($\ell, \ell'=e, μ, τ$). These are related to one another at high energies by the electroweak $SU(2)$ gauge symmetry, and therefore probe different components of the same underlying electroweak dynamics. These processes open qualitatively new windows to neutrino interactions at unprecedented energies, and open new windows on physics beyond the Standard Model. We demonstrate the combined sensitivity and the complementarity of the different purely-leptonic collisions using both effective field theory and simplified models of $W'^\pm$ and $Z'$ vector bosons. The radiative return mechanism leads to resonant production of the $W^{\prime\pm}$ and $Z'$ vector bosons when their masses are kinematically accessible, while their off-shell effects are captured by the corresponding effective operators. In particular, we show that supplementing the dominant charged-lepton collisions with neutrino-initiated collisions significantly improves the ability to resolve degeneracies among different effective operators, even for operators to which the neutrino-initiated processes are not directly sensitive. Including these channels is therefore essential for properly characterizing the structure of new physics. △ Less

    Submitted 27 October, 2026; originally announced October 2026.

    Comments: 30 pages + Appendices

    Report number: COMETA-2026-44,IRMP-CP3-26-33,PITT-PACC-2615

The nonuniqueness mechanism is a cascade of transitions in quadrupole dynamics across infinitely many scales. ▽ More We construct two distinct solutions of the two-dimensional incompressible Euler equation with the same initial vorticity in $\bigcap_{1\le p<\infty}L^p(\mathbb R^2)$. The vorticities are compactly supported in space and have mean zero, so the corresponding velocities have finite energy. The nonuniqueness mechanism is a cascade of transitions in quadrupole dynamics across infinitely many scales. △ Less

    cs.CV

    Repurposing Video Diffusion Transformers for Cross-View Temporal Object Correspondence

    Authors: Siyoon Jin, Dahyun Chung, Honggyu An, Sangbeom Lim, Wonjin Nam, Jaewoo Jung, WonJun Moon, Seungryong Kim

    Abstract: Cross-view object correspondence identifies the same object across views. However, existing methods often rely on frame-wise matching or sequential tracking, which can propagate… ▽ More Cross-view object correspondence identifies the same object across views. However, existing methods often rely on frame-wise matching or sequential tracking, which can propagate correspondence errors under appearance changes or occlusions. Therefore, we introduce cross-view temporal object correspondence, which requires preserving object identity across both views and time from a query mask. To address this, we propose XTrack, a unified framework which repurposes a pretrained video DiT with semantic and spatiotemporal priors for RGB-to-mask latent transport, jointly modeling cross-view matching and temporal tracking. However, naive video DiTs do not guarantee accurate cross-view grounding and often attend to semantically similar yet incorrect objects across views. Thus, we introduce focal cross-view attention alignment, hard negative conditioning, and local context crop strategies to ensure reliable grounding. Finally, beyond these architectural enhancements, we also tackle the lack of dense annotation by introducing a pseudo-label curation pipeline that converts sparse Ego-Exo4D annotations into dense supervision to enable effective video-level training. Our results demonstrate the effectiveness of XTrack as a unified framework, ensuring robust temporal consistency and identity preservation. △ Less

    Submitted 27 October, 2026; originally announced October 2026.

    Comments: Project Page : https://cvlab-kaist.github.io/XTrack/

    Lower- and upper-$C^2$ integrands: a structured class of functions for stochastic nonconvex optimization

    Authors: Felipe Atenas, Alejandro Jofré, Pedro Pérez-Aros, David Torregrosa-Belén

    Abstract: …theory follows by a change of sign, and delivers the corresponding lower envelopes, the form in which these integrands appear as second-stage value functions of stochastic programs with compact recourse. We also show that for this class integrability of the function values, of the local Lipschitz moduli, and of the minimum-norm Clarke subgradients are equiv… ▽ More We study nonconvex stochastic functions that admit a structured second-order representation, a generalization of lower- and upper-$C^2$ functions to normal integrands. Similarly to the deterministic case, lower-$C^2$ normal integrands are defined as the pointwise maxima over a compact random index set of functions that are twice-continuously differentiable in the decision variable with an integrable bounded Hessian. Our main result is a local value function characterization of this class of functions, namely, that they satisfy a local sufficient ascent property almost surely. Equivalently, they differ from a convex normal integrand by a random quadratic. The upper-$C^2$ theory follows by a change of sign, and delivers the corresponding lower envelopes, the form in which these integrands appear as second-stage value functions of stochastic programs with compact recourse. We also show that for this class integrability of the function values, of the local Lipschitz moduli, and of the minimum-norm Clarke subgradients are equivalent. We then study smooth integrands, finite lower envelopes, difference-of-convex integrands, Moreau and forward--backward envelopes, compositions with a smooth map, monotone convex compositions, and compact-recourse stochastic programs within these classes of nonconvex stochastic functions. As an application, we show that the optimistic loss of partial-label regression and the variational formulation of the conditional value-at-risk fit within this framework. △ Less

    Linear-quadratic mean field games with Volterra dynamics

    Authors: Niccolò Fontana, Mattia Martini, Huyên Pham

    Abstract: …horizons and, when the control does not act on the diffusion coefficient, on arbitrary horizons under a monotonicity condition on the interaction. Finally, we consider the corresponding finite-player game and establish a quantitative propagation of chaos result. As a consequence, the feedback control of any mean field equilibrium, when implemented by each ag… ▽ More We study a class of linear-quadratic mean field games in which the state of each agent follows a stochastic Volterra equation of convolution type. The resulting dynamics are non-Markovian and account for memory effects through a completely monotone kernel, while the mean field interaction enters both the drift and diffusion coefficients. Exploiting a Markovian lifting of the Volterra dynamics, which restores the Markov property at the price of moving to an infinite-dimensional state space, we characterize the optimal response of a representative agent for a given population mean through a system of Riccati equations in Banach spaces, thereby obtaining a feedback representation of the optimal control. We then study the consistency condition defining the mean field equilibrium, which reduces to a forward-backward fixed point equation for the equilibrium mean: we prove existence and uniqueness of the equilibrium on short horizons and, when the control does not act on the diffusion coefficient, on arbitrary horizons under a monotonicity condition on the interaction. Finally, we consider the corresponding finite-player game and establish a quantitative propagation of chaos result. As a consequence, the feedback control of any mean field equilibrium, when implemented by each agent on its own state, yields an $\varepsilon_N$-Nash equilibrium for the $N$-player game, with an explicit rate as the number of agents tends to infinity. △ Less

    cs.LG

    Sparse Attention as Compact Kernel Regression

    Authors: Saul Santos, Nuno Gonçalves, Daniel C. McNamee, Marcos Treviso, André F. T Martins

    Abstract: …work has revealed a link between self-attention mechanisms in transformers and test-time kernel regression via the Nadaraya-Watson estimator, with standard softmax attention corresponding to a Gaussian kernel. However, a kernel-theoretic understanding of sparse attention mechanisms is currently missing. In this paper, we establish a formal… ▽ More Recent work has revealed a link between self-attention mechanisms in transformers and test-time kernel regression via the Nadaraya-Watson estimator, with standard softmax attention corresponding to a Gaussian kernel. However, a kernel-theoretic understanding of sparse attention mechanisms is currently missing. In this paper, we establish a formal correspondence between sparse attention and compact (bounded support) kernels. We show that normalized ReLU and sparsemax attention arise from Epanechnikov kernel regression under fixed and adaptive normalizations, respectively. More generally, we demonstrate that widely used kernels in nonparametric density estimation -- including Epanechnikov, biweight, and triweight -- correspond to $α$-entmax attention with $α= 1 + \frac{1}{n}$ for $n \in \mathbb{N}$, while the softmax/Gaussian relationship emerges in the limit $n \to \infty$. This unified perspective explains how sparsity naturally emerges from kernel design and provides principled alternatives to heuristic top-$k$ attention and other associative memory mechanisms. Experiments with a kernel-regression-based variant of transformers -- Memory Mosaics -- show that kernel-based sparse attention achieves competitive performance on language modeling, in-context learning, and length generalization tasks, offering a principled framework for designing attention mechanisms. △ Less

    Compactifications of Abelian Group Bundles and a Topological Halmos--von Neumann-type Theorem

    Authors: Dustin Anglewitz, Patrick Hermle, Henrik Kreidler

    Abstract: …result of topological dynamics that completely classifies continuous actions of abelian topological groups which are ergodic and have discrete spectrum. It rests on a correspondence between such actions (up to the choice of a distinguished point), compactifications of the acting group and subgroups of its dual group. We generalize both the classical statem… ▽ More The topological Halmos--von Neumann theorem is a fundamental result of topological dynamics that completely classifies continuous actions of abelian topological groups which are ergodic and have discrete spectrum. It rests on a correspondence between such actions (up to the choice of a distinguished point), compactifications of the acting group and subgroups of its dual group. We generalize both the classical statement and the underlying categorical equivalences to the framework of open group bundles over locally compact base spaces. As a byproduct, we establish a Pontryagin-type duality between étale Hausdorff abelian group bundles and open proper Hausdorff abelian group bundles, extending the classical duality between discrete and compact abelian groups. △ Less

    Cutting rules in strong field QED with application to trident pair production

    Authors: Y. V. Selivanov, A. A. Mironov, A. I. Alexeenko, A. M. Fedotov

    Abstract: Following Veltman's approach, we formulate and discuss a general cutting equation for QED in a plane-wave background. We apply the corresponding cutting rules to justify the connection between the two-loop radiative corrections to elastic electron scattering and the rate of the trident process in a constant crossed field. Among other terms generated by c… ▽ More Following Veltman's approach, we formulate and discuss a general cutting equation for QED in a plane-wave background. We apply the corresponding cutting rules to justify the connection between the two-loop radiative corrections to elastic electron scattering and the rate of the trident process in a constant crossed field. Among other terms generated by cuttings, some field-induced radiation corrections also emerge, which contain UV-finite contributions from loop insertions into external legs. As a byproduct, we compare the previously published results for the trident process in a constant crossed field and present a complete analytical expression for direct and exchange contributions to its rate, which is resolved in the spin of the initial electron. Our findings establish that although total rates can be reliably extracted from higher-loop by applying the cutting rules, reconstruction of differential rates requires additional care. The cutting rules apply to any loop order and may be extended to nonperturbative regimes. △ Less

    Finite-size corrections to the crosscap overlap in the two-dimensional Ising model

    Authors: Yiteng Zhang, Li-Ping Yang, Hong-Hao Tu, Yueshui Zhang

    Abstract: …for the crosscap overlap, which demonstrates that the finite-size corrections decay exponentially with system size. We further derive an exact analytical formula for the corresponding decay constant and show that it is determined by the complex singularity structure of the Bogoliubov angle. ▽ More We analyze the finite-size corrections to the crosscap overlap in the two-dimensional classical Ising model along its self-dual critical line. Using a fermionic formulation, we express the lattice crosscap overlap in terms of Bogoliubov angles and develop a contour-integral approach by analytically continuing the lattice momentum to the complex plane. This leads to a remarkably simple expression for the crosscap overlap, which demonstrates that the finite-size corrections decay exponentially with system size. We further derive an exact analytical formula for the corresponding decay constant and show that it is determined by the complex singularity structure of the Bogoliubov angle. △ Less

    RESPLE2: Inertial-Informed Recursive Non-Uniform Spline Estimation for Multi-Sensor Odometry with Global Refinement

    Authors: Ziyu Cao, William Talbot, Song Li, Pakpong Chirarattananon, Fredrik Gustafsson, Marco Hutter, Kailai Li

    Abstract: …with extreme accelerations. Compared with state-of-the-art multi-sensor odometry systems, RESPLE2 achieves competitive or superior estimation accuracy and robustness in the corresponding sensor configurations, particularly under aggressive motion, while maintaining real-time operation. We publicly release our code and experimental datasets at https://github.… ▽ More We present RESPLE2, a recursive multi-sensor state estimation framework using non-uniform B-splines for 6-DoF motion representation. By exploiting the knot-clamping boundary property, we propose a theoretically grounded mechanism for exact control-point prediction directly informed by inertial kinematics along the trajectory, with knot placement adapted to motion and measurement availability. Within an iterated extended Kalman filter, we perform this prediction using a subset of inertial measurements. A subsequent reset step preserves trajectory consistency as the knots extend. The update step further incorporates heterogeneous sensor measurements at their native timestamps through continuous-time observation models, supporting flexible combinations of LiDARs, cameras, inertial measurement units, and leg kinematics. We further develop a lightweight global optimization module that directly refines non-uniform B-spline control points by incorporating loop-closure constraints derived from LiDAR-visual cues. We conduct extensive evaluations on public and experimental datasets across handheld, tracked, legged, and hybrid aerial-legged platforms, covering highly dynamic maneuvers with extreme accelerations. Compared with state-of-the-art multi-sensor odometry systems, RESPLE2 achieves competitive or superior estimation accuracy and robustness in the corresponding sensor configurations, particularly under aggressive motion, while maintaining real-time operation. We publicly release our code and experimental datasets at https://github.com/ASIG-X/RESPLE2. △ Less

    Search for Neutrino-Produced Long-Lived Particles Using the SBND Cosmic Ray Tagger Beam Telescope

Bhat, M. Bishai, A. Blake, B. Bogart, D. Brailsford, A. Brandt, S. Brickner, M. B. Brunetti, G. A. N. Buzzard, L. Camilleri, D. Caratelli, D. Carber, B. Carlson , et al. (166 additional authors not shown)

    Abstract: …in December 2024, a subset of the Cosmic Ray Tagger (CRT) system operated from 2017--2018 as a standalone detector, referred to as the CRT Beam Telescope, collecting data corresponding to $2.95\times 10^{20}$ protons on target from the BNB. Originally intended for commissioning and performance studies, the substantial dataset collected also enables searches… ▽ More The Short-Baseline Near Detector (SBND) is a liquid argon time projection chamber in Fermilab's Short-Baseline Neutrino Program, positioned 110 meters from the neutrino target along the Booster Neutrino Beam (BNB). In addition to its neutrino physics goals, SBND enables searches for new physics, including long-lived particles (LLPs) produced via neutrino-nucleus upscattering that decay into visible final states. Although neutrino data taking with the full SBND experiment began in December 2024, a subset of the Cosmic Ray Tagger (CRT) system operated from 2017--2018 as a standalone detector, referred to as the CRT Beam Telescope, collecting data corresponding to $2.95\times 10^{20}$ protons on target from the BNB. Originally intended for commissioning and performance studies, the substantial dataset collected also enables searches for displaced LLP decays. This paper presents the first search for physics beyond the Standard Model using data from the SBND CRT Beam Telescope, targeting LLP decays into electron-positron pairs. We observe no significant excess above the expected background and set 90% confidence-level upper limits on an effective production cross section at the level of $10^{-38}$ cm$^2$ for laboratory decay lengths of 0.3-100 m. These results complement existing searches by probing displaced decay signatures at meter-scale decay lengths. △ Less

    Rendering-Free Event Frame Simulation for Fast Reinforcement Learning of Autonomous Robot Navigation

    Authors: Dequan Ou, Austin Lopez, Quentin Missinne, Christophe De Wagter, Guido C. H. E. de Croon

    Abstract: …propose an analytic event simulator that exploits what event cameras fundamentally measure - moving contrast edges - to synthesize event streams directly from scene geometry and ego-motion without visual rendering. Our simulator generates a 64x64 event observation in 17 microseconds on a single GPU - over 400x faster t… ▽ More Event cameras offer low latency, high dynamic range, and milliwatt-level power consumption, making them ideal for agile robots such as tiny drones navigating through cluttered environments. However, existing simulators generally render photorealistic images before converting them into events, making large-scale reinforcement learning prohibitively slow. We propose an analytic event simulator that exploits what event cameras fundamentally measure - moving contrast edges - to synthesize event streams directly from scene geometry and ego-motion without visual rendering. Our simulator generates a 64x64 event observation in 17 microseconds on a single GPU - over 400x faster than a state-of-the-art rendering pipeline - enabling end-to-end reinforcement learning for goal-directed obstacle avoidance to converge in under 30 minutes - three orders of magnitude faster than training on rendered events. Policies transfer zero-shot to physically based event simulations (ray-traced Isaac Sim). Our policy reaches 83% success in cluttered courses unseen during training (mid-difficulty, traversability probability approx. 0.57), at 2.4 m/s average goal velocity, and retains 70% in a simulated texture-dense indoor hall. Furthermore, we quantized the policy to a 1.2 MB int8 network running fully onboard a 56-gram Crazyflie equipped with a Prophesee GenX320 event camera and an OpenMV N6 microcontroller board. Without any fine-tuning, external processing, or positioning, the policy achieves an average success rate of 82% (5/5, 5/6, 4/6 flights) across three real-world obstacle courses of increasing difficulty. Code: https://github.com/tudelft/rendering-free-event-nav △ Less

    Walshness: an intrinsic neural-network representability metric for quantum states

    Authors: Nisarga Paul, Kehuang Chen, Jessica K. Jiang, Haimeng Zhao, Di Luo

    Abstract: …if it has bounded Walshness, and vice versa, for various NQS architectures. We provide an algorithm for finding the optimal basis by minimizing the energy of a corresponding classical spin model. This allows us to empirically improve the learnability of various physical quantum states, including ground states of the transverse-field Ising model and mixed-fie… ▽ More Neural quantum states (NQS) have emerged as powerful representations of quantum states with rapidly expanding applications across quantum many-body physics. Yet our understanding of when neural networks can efficiently represent physical quantum states remains limited, in part due to the nonlinear parameterization of NQS, the intricate sign structure of quantum states, and the sensitive dependence on basis choice. We introduce Walshness, a complexity metric of a quantum state which quantifies whether it admits a compact neural-network representation. We rigorously prove that a general quantum many-body state admits an efficient NQS representation if it has bounded Walshness, and vice versa, for various NQS architectures. We provide an algorithm for finding the optimal basis by minimizing the energy of a corresponding classical spin model. This allows us to empirically improve the learnability of various physical quantum states, including ground states of the transverse-field Ising model and mixed-field toric code, often reducing infidelity by orders of magnitude, and generalizes the basis choice given by the well-known Marshall sign rule in frustrated antiferromagnets. Our results provide an intrinsic complexity metric for quantum states with provable connections to their neural-network representability. △ Less

    Correspondence Is Not Transfer: A Pairing-Controlled Study of Clinical Audio-Metadata Alignment

    Authors: Liu He, Xi Wang, Jiahong Yuan, Yuanchao Li

    Abstract: …Sounds, and Coswara, where retrieval and sex-probe contrasts are consistently positive but disease contrasts are not. We find no consistent evidence that better profile correspondence translates into better disease prediction and suggest reporting these outcomes separately. ▽ More Clinical audio models are often trained by aligning recordings with participant metadata or text derived from it. This metadata is rarely a clean disease signal, since a single profile also contains symptoms, age, sex, missing responses, and recruitment information. We examine what alignment adds to frozen audio features by varying only the audio-meta data pairing. We keep the audio backbones fixed and train only a projector under correct pairing and three shuffled conditions that progressively preserve the disease label and recorded sex. We then evaluate each projector on profile retrieval, attribute probing, and disease classification on covariate-matched test sets. On UKCOVID, correct pairing clearly improves retrieval and makes sex easier to decode, confirming that the pairing manipulation works. Most of the sex-probe gap disappears when the control also preserves recorded sex, while a small retrieval gap remains. Matched disease AUROC differences are small, and every interval includes zero, so modest benefits or harms are not ruled out. The same pattern appears when we rerun the frozen pipeline on CODA TB, Cambridge COVID-19 Sounds, and Coswara, where retrieval and sex-probe contrasts are consistently positive but disease contrasts are not. We find no consistent evidence that better profile correspondence translates into better disease prediction and suggest reporting these outcomes separately. △ Less

    Abstract: …examining the commensurability of the radial and azimuthal orbital frequencies. For representative trajectories, we numerically reconstruct the particle motion and calculate corresponding gravitational wave polarizations using the symmetric trace-free mass-quadrupole approach. By comparing these signals with their Schwarzschild counterparts, we assess how th… ▽ More We investigate how deviations from the Schwarzschild geometry affect the motion of massive test particles and the gravitational radiation associated with their periodic trajectories around a new improved Schwarzschild black hole. Periodic configurations are determined by examining the commensurability of the radial and azimuthal orbital frequencies. For representative trajectories, we numerically reconstruct the particle motion and calculate corresponding gravitational wave polarizations using the symmetric trace-free mass-quadrupole approach. By comparing these signals with their Schwarzschild counterparts, we assess how the modified spacetime geometry is encoded in the radiation. Our analysis shows that the parameters $ξ$ and $γ$ produce modest changes in the orbital configurations and their associated waveforms, providing a means of investigating the dynamical signatures of this BH model. △ Less

    cs.IT

    The Minimum Subpacketization of Reed-Solomon Codes with Linear Repair Achieving the Cut-Set Bound

    Authors: Weijun Fang, Jing Qiu, Shu-Tao Xia, Fang-Wei Fu

    Abstract: …, resolving the corresponding exact linear-repair case of Open Problem 20 posed by Ramkumar et al. in their survey on codes for distributed storage. We further give a finite formula for $G_s(n)$ and determine the exact threshold for its closed-form expression in terms of the first $n$ primes. For fixed $k$ and $d$, we prove… ▽ More In this paper, we study the minimum subpacketization of $(n,k)$ Reed-Solomon (RS) codes over $\mathbb{F}_{q^\ell}$ admitting cut-set-optimal exact $\mathbb{F}_q$-linear repair of every coordinate from any $d$ helpers. For $3\le k<d\le n-1$, write $s=d+1-k$ and denote this minimum by $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)$. Using divisibility and relative-degree constraints on cross-ratio fields of evaluation sets, we establish $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)\ge sG_s(d+1)$, where $G_s(m)$ is the minimum product of $m$ pairwise coprime integers, each at least $s$. Let $Δ_s(n,d)$ be the minimum of $lcm(a_1,\ldots,a_n)$ over positive integers $a_1,\ldots,a_n$ satisfying $lcm(a_j:j\in J\cup\{i\})\ge s~lcm(a_j:j\in J)$ for every $d$-subset $J\subseteq[n]$ and every $i\in[n]\setminus J$. We construct RS codes with subpacketization $sΔ_s(n,d)$ and further prove that $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)=sΔ_s(n,d)=sG_s(n)$ whenever $d(d+2)\geq 4(n+1)$, for every prime power $q$. In particular, this determines the minimum subpacketization in the full-helper case $d=n-1$, resolving the corresponding exact linear-repair case of Open Problem 20 posed by Ramkumar et al. in their survey on codes for distributed storage. We further give a finite formula for $G_s(n)$ and determine the exact threshold for its closed-form expression in terms of the first $n$ primes. For fixed $k$ and $d$, we prove $\log\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)= Θ_{k,d}(\log n\log\log n)$ as $n\to\infty$, using divisor-function estimates and cover-free families. An affine-plane construction shows that $Δ_s(n,d)<G_s(n)$ for some parameters, so $sG_s(n)$ is not a universal formula. We conjecture that $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)=sΔ_s(n,d)$ for every prime power $q$ and all $3\le k<d\le n-1$. △ Less

    World Dreamer v5-lite: A Multi-Robot World Action Model with One Dreamer

    Authors: Guoning Wu, Zijian Cai, Yuhang Zhang, Ying Liu, Yongbin Zheng

    Abstract: …while predicting separate action sequences. Most vision-language-action and world-action models target one robot. Most vision-language-action and world-action models target one robot. Combining multiple views alone does not establish spatial correspondence between them. World Dreamer v5-lite is a multi-robot world-action model built around a shared world state for robot-specific action prediction. Calibrated features from a pretrained video encoder populate this state in workspace coordinates; its spatial size remains fixed as views are added. A vision-language model provides scene context and one query per robot. The queries read the shared state and exchange pose-aware information, while their action streams remain separate. Masked-view cooperative training withholds selected camera views from both visual branches while retaining all action targets. This encourages the use of observations shared by other robots. Pretraining uses more than 10,000 hours of trajectories from 20 simulated robot types, RoboFactory, and real robots. On RoboFactory, World Dreamer v5-lite achieves 74.0% mean success, 23.7 percentage points above the strongest evaluated baseline. Across four real-robot tasks, it reaches 78.8% mean success, a margin of 13.8 points. It also reaches 58.08% overall on RoboCasa365. △ Less

    Authors: Asit Kumar Pradhan, Nithin Raveendran, David Declercq, Bane Vasić

    Abstract: …of modeling each such fault with an explicit correlation node and relying on OSD to clean up the resulting failures, we design message-passing decoders that resolve the corresponding \emph{stabilizer-induced} TSs directly. We obtain these decoders by deriving QLDPC decoders from decoders for the parent classical LDPC codes and using them collectively to corr… ▽ More We design low-complexity, fault-tolerant decoders for quantum low-density parity-check (QLDPC) codes with the goal of reducing decoding latency. We target two major bottlenecks of decoding under the \emph{circuit-level} noise model: (i) post-processing via order-statistics decoding (OSD), and (ii) the large number of auxiliary variable nodes commonly introduced to represent CNOT-induced correlations during syndrome extraction. Our key observation is that propagating CNOT faults (\emph{hook errors}) create \emph{stabilizer-induced} trapping sets (TSs) that are intrinsic to hypergraph-product (HGP) and lifted-product (LP) constructions. Therefore, instead of modeling each such fault with an explicit correlation node and relying on OSD to clean up the resulting failures, we design message-passing decoders that resolve the corresponding \emph{stabilizer-induced} TSs directly. We obtain these decoders by deriving QLDPC decoders from decoders for the parent classical LDPC codes and using them collectively to correct broad families of \emph{stabilizer-induced} TSs. For CNOT faults that manifest primarily as syndrome errors, we show that their effect is equivalent to a data error together with syndrome-bit measurement errors. Consequently, given repeated measurements and a decoding graph that already includes nodes representing syndrome-bit errors, no distinct variable node is needed for each CNOT fault. Using a \emph{phenomenological} Tanner graph with nodes representing only data errors and syndrome-bit errors, simulations on LP codes show a significant improvement in logical error rate relative to BP+OSD. Moreover, the improved error correction relies on low-complexity finite-state iterative decoders, targeted for fast and low-latency FPGA implementation. △ Less

    Authors: Devon Yanitski, Chris Westbury

    Abstract: …and compares the results with a factor analysis of 874,434 respondents. Retention criteria tied between five and six factors, and the tie rule kept five. Each factor's congruence with its best-matching human factor (1 = identical) ranged f… ▽ More The items of a reflective psychometric scale can be read as a proposal about a construct's structure. Language models make that proposal measurable by converting each item into an embedding, a list of numbers, similar for similar meanings. We introduce semanticfa, an R package for semantic factor analysis, which factors item-embedding similarities. It also interprets factors, flags redundant items, builds short forms, and screens new items. A worked example analyzes the 50-item International Personality Item Pool Big-Five Factor Markers, embedded with Qwen3-Embedding-8B, and compares the results with a factor analysis of 874,434 respondents. Retention criteria tied between five and six factors, and the tie rule kept five. Three corresponded mainly to single domains, and two blended most Extraversion items with five Agreeableness items. Each factor's congruence with its best-matching human factor (1 = identical) ranged from .92 for Openness to .52 for Agreeableness. Item pairs more similar in meaning tended to be answered more alike (r = .46 with raw responses), but same-domain reverse-keyed opposites, answered oppositely, were about as similar as cross-domain pairs (.58 vs. .57). Sign-flipping reverse-keyed items' embeddings did not improve agreement with raw responses and worsened it with reverse-scored ones. With five factors, a 25-item short form matched the domains better than all 200 random five-per-domain sets, but parallel analysis kept only three. We propose semantic-behavioral coherence, the agreement between semantic and response structures, as a measurable property of a scale, given an embedding model. Semantic factor analysis complements, and does not replace, response-based factor analysis. △ Less

    Vortex Dynamics Driven Transverse Transport in Superconducting Thin Films

    Authors: Laxmipriya Nanda, Sohini Guin, Yasen Hou, Rajib Sarkar, Naresh Shyaga, Souvik Banerjee, A. Sundaresan, N. S. Vidhyadhiraja, Jagadeesh S. Moodera, Dhavala Suri

    Abstract: …motion of Abrikosov vortices under a combined action of Magnus like force and viscous drag force, rather than from any exotic or intrinsic Hall-type mechanism. The close correspondence between a nominally conventional superconductor Nb and an unconventional superconductor heterostructure incorporating a ferromagnetic layer indicates that the vortex dynamics… ▽ More The generation of transverse voltage in superconducting thin films carrying a longitudinal current has been recently related to demonstrate exotic, symmetry-breaking transport phenomena. To understand the underlying phenomenon we chose two distinctly different systems to investigate; a conventional (Nb) and a nonconventional (Ni/Bi bilayer) superconductor. A systematic study of this transverse response in these type-II superconducting thin films we aimed towards establishing its microscopic origin. Both longitudinal and transverse magneto transport measurements were carried out with Hall-bar patterned devices as a function of temperature with magnetic field applied out-of-plane. In both systems, a sharp, field antisymmetric transverse resistance emerges in the superconducting transition region, closely following the field derivative of the longitudinal resistance while it was vanishingly small or absent in the fully superconducting or normal states. Based on the vortex-flow model, the behavior could be quantitatively attributed as the transverse voltage arising due to the motion of Abrikosov vortices under a combined action of Magnus like force and viscous drag force, rather than from any exotic or intrinsic Hall-type mechanism. The close correspondence between a nominally conventional superconductor Nb and an unconventional superconductor heterostructure incorporating a ferromagnetic layer indicates that the vortex dynamics alone, independent of magnetic proximity effects or unconventional pairing, seems to be sufficient to account for the observed transverse signal. Our observations while seems to provide a quantitative, material-independent model for interpreting transverse voltages in superconducting films, raises the concerns in attributing such signals to exotic physics without first ruling out conventional vortex-flow contributions. △ Less

    Abstract: …Via the Cayley trick, for any disjunction of full-dimensional polytopes, the facets of the convex hull other than those described by the bounds on the indicator variables correspond to the facet normals of the Minkowski sum of the polytopes, and each is described by the full optimal big-M lifting of the supporting inequality with that normal of any one poly… ▽ More Disjunctions over the cells of a triangulation arise in formulations of piecewise-linear functions. For a poset $P$ on $[d]$, we study the convex hull $D$ of the simplices of Stanley's triangulation of the order polytope of $P$, each lifted by its own indicator vector. Via the Cayley trick, for any disjunction of full-dimensional polytopes, the facets of the convex hull other than those described by the bounds on the indicator variables correspond to the facet normals of the Minkowski sum of the polytopes, and each is described by the full optimal big-M lifting of the supporting inequality with that normal of any one polytope. For Stanley's triangulation, with $M(P)$ the Minkowski sum of its simplices, lifting only the facet-describing inequalities of the simplices need not suffice, unlike some other cases; we show that it suffices if and only if $M(P)$ is alcoved, if and only if deleting one element of $P$ leaves a chain. Otherwise, the coefficients of the additional facet-describing inequalities are governed by the least number $\ell(P)$ of elements whose deletion leaves a chain: for fixed $\ell(P)$, the largest entry of a primitive facet normal grows like $d^{\ell(P)-1}$, and it can be exponential in $d$ if $\ell(P)$ grows with $d$. For $d \geq 3$ and $\ell(P)\leq2$ the sharp bound is $d-2$. △ Less

Although image-caption pre-training improves perception, its influence can weaken as subsequent stages change data mixtures and objectives.     On aggregation-quantization permutability problem for discrete-time Markov chains

    Authors: Adam Doliwa, Artur Siemaszko, Adam Zalewski

    Abstract: Given random walk on a graph, the corresponding discrete-time quantum walk can be constructed using the method proposed by Szegedy. On the other hand, given a partition of the set of states of a Markov chain, one can study the corresponding aggregated process. We extend the aggregation technique to the level of quantum… ▽ More Given random walk on a graph, the corresponding discrete-time quantum walk can be constructed using the method proposed by Szegedy. On the other hand, given a partition of the set of states of a Markov chain, one can study the corresponding aggregated process. We extend the aggregation technique to the level of quantum Markov chains. We provide conditions under which application of these two operations - Szegedy's quantization and aggregation - give the same result. These consist of the strong lumpability of the underlying Markov chain supplemented by nonlinear relations, generalizing Kolmogorov's cycle condition, that account for the coefficients of both the original and the aggregated transition matrices. In particular, we show that the conditions are satisfied in the case of the random walk on graphs equipped with equitable partitions. We present several examples, which include the classical/quantum walks on Platonic solids. We discuss also relation of discrete-time classical/quantum walks on $N$-dimensional hypercube and the Ehrenfests urn model with $N$ particles. We apply our technique for of discrete-time walks on Cayley graphs of free groups. We also compare our results with those obtained using Cantero-Moral-Velazquez uniformization of unitary matrices. △ Less

Our result is based on a novel extended formulation, recursively constructed using deformed products. Its key feature is that it projects onto a polygonal approximation of a parabola while preserving all of its exponentially many vertices. Each pairwise marginal of MSC has the same law as the corresponding split-coupled pair, allowing existing variance bounds to transfer directly; in finite-state settings, we also obtain first-order expansions for the mean and second moment of… ▽ More Stochastic reaction networks are continuous-time Markov chain models for interacting populations, with applications in biochemistry, epidemiology, ecology, and related areas. Coupling multiple stochastic reaction networks effectively is a fundamental challenge in stochastic simulation. Existing variance-reducing coupling methods are typically designed for pairs of processes, though specialized multi-path constructions have been developed for particular applications. To fill this gap, we introduce the multi-path stacked coupling (MSC), a space-time Poisson construction that jointly simulates a finite collection of stochastic reaction networks with common reaction channels and reaction vectors, but possibly different intensity functions and initial conditions. Each pairwise marginal of MSC has the same law as the corresponding split-coupled pair, allowing existing variance bounds to transfer directly; in finite-state settings, we also obtain first-order expansions for the mean and second moment of finite-difference numerators. We demonstrate the framework through three sensitivity estimation tasks requiring several nearby parameterized paths: estimating many first derivatives simultaneously, estimating a single first derivative using a wider finite-difference stencil, and estimating higher-order derivatives. Numerical experiments on a processive phosphorylation network demonstrate strong performance in each of the three application areas considered, consistent with the theoretical advantages of MSC: across all three applications, MSC achieves the smallest root mean square error (RMSE) among the methods considered over the tested computational budgets. △ Less

We also provide logical and com… ▽ More The von Neumann measurement formulation of quantum phase estimation [Phys.\ Rev.\ A \textbf{66}, 032314 (2002); Quantum \textbf{5}, 465 (2021)] estimates energies by coupling the system to a discretized pointer register through $\hat H\otimes\hat p$ and reading out the pointer after an inverse quantum Fourier transform. Building on these works, we develop a tensor-network framework for simulating and benchmarking this protocol when the system is initialized in a matrix product state (MPS) pre-trained with the density matrix renormalization group (DMRG). The system and the pointer are represented as a composite MPS, the coupling $\hat H\otimes\hat p$ as a matrix product operator, and the coupled dynamics is evolved with the time-dependent variational principle. This gives direct access to the time-resolved pointer distribution, the recovered energy, and the entanglement generated between system and pointer. We apply the framework to quantum many-body spin systems and to electronic-structure problems. For the systems studied, the pointer readout yields accurate ground-state energy estimates from DMRG-prepared approximate states, even when their variational energies are not fully converged. These states generally contain contributions from both ground and excited eigenstates, with their spectral weights determining the corresponding contributions to the pointer distribution. More accurate initial states also strongly suppress the entanglement generated between system and pointer. We also provide logical and compiled resource estimates for a Suzuki--Trotter realization of the protocol. △ Less

    Generalizable Target-Less Joint Intrinsic-Extrinsic Camera-LiDAR Calibration using Deep Pixel-Point Correspondences

    Authors: Simon Bultmann, Daniele Cattaneo, Abhinav Valada

    Abstract: Accurate camera-LiDAR calibration is an essential prerequisite for robust multi-modal perception in robotics. However, they operate on rectified images with known camera intrinsics, restricting their applicab… ▽ More Accurate camera-LiDAR calibration is an essential prerequisite for robust multi-modal perception in robotics. Recent target-less methods based on learned pixel-point correspondences achieve high extrinsic calibration accuracy and generalize across environments. However, they operate on rectified images with known camera intrinsics, restricting their applicability to systems whose cameras have already been individually calibrated. We present a fully automatic, target-less framework that jointly estimates camera intrinsics, radial-tangential distortion, and camera-LiDAR extrinsics from raw images and LiDAR scans. Our method generalizes deep correspondence-based calibration from rectified images to raw cameras through intrinsic- and distortion-aware correspondence learning via synthetic augmentations. During calibration, we iteratively couple correspondence prediction and nonlinear optimization to jointly refine the camera parameters and relative sensor transformation. We evaluate our approach on an unseen camera-LiDAR configuration from the KITTI dataset and on three real robotic platforms in a zero-shot manner. The results demonstrate that joint calibration with our generalized pixel-point correspondence network improves extrinsic accuracy over correspondence-based methods using pre-calibrated cameras, while additionally recovering accurate intrinsics. △ Less

    Uneven Use, Similar Trust Generative AI in Denmark and Romania

    Authors: Iulian Lucau, Adelin-George Voicu, Simon Munk Christensen

    Abstract: …trust. This paper compares Denmark and Romania, the EU's highest and lowest GenAI users, and asks whether the difference in public and institutional trust between them corresponds to the difference in trust in AI. Drawing on trust, technology-acceptance and digital-divide theory, it is a descriptive, contrasting-case analysis of harmonised data: GenAI us… ▽ More Generative AI (GenAI) has spread unevenly across Europe, and it is unclear whether higher use brings higher trust. This paper compares Denmark and Romania, the EU's highest and lowest GenAI users, and asks whether the difference in public and institutional trust between them corresponds to the difference in trust in AI. Drawing on trust, technology-acceptance and digital-divide theory, it is a descriptive, contrasting-case analysis of harmonised data: GenAI usage comes from Eurostat, trust in AI more generally from the KPMG/Melbourne global study and public and institutional trust from the Standard Eurobarometer. In 2025, 48.4% of Danes aged 16-74 had used GenAI in the past three months, against 17.8% of Romanians. Use falls with age and rises with education in both countries, and the relative gaps are far larger in Romania; the patterns remain visible within the published age-by-education categories. Trust in AI does not follow usage: Romanians trust AI as much as Danes or slightly more, although Denmark leads by 28 to 45 percentage points on trust in government, parliament and the legal system. The Denmark-Romania comparison thus does not support a simple country-level correspondence between general institutional trust and trust in AI; in KPMG's pooled model, trust in AI is tied most closely to confidence in AI governance. The observed usage difference is more consistent with disparities in general digital skills and opportunities for work- and education-related use than with differences in trust. However, the available data cannot identify the independent effects of these factors. The results rest on two cases and non-identical constructs, and call for comparable microdata and disclosure experiments. △ Less

The CONUS+ experiment, located at the Leibstadt nuclear power plant, reported the first observation of CE$ν$NS using reactor antineutrinos in 2025. Following a detector upgrade that replaced three of the original 1 kg high-purity germanium detectors with larger 2.42 kg diodes, this paper presents the analysis of the CONUS+ run 2 dataset, including the detector characterization, the background model, the signal prediction and expected sensitivity, and the extraction of the CE$ν$NS signal.     Geodesic dynamics and tidal forces in a quantum-regularized black hole from string T-duality

    Authors: G. Alencar, Micael S. Oliveira, Diego Sáez-Chillón Gómez, Marcos V. de S. Silva

    Abstract: …determines the transition between black hole and horizonless geometries. We then study the motion of massive and massless particles through the corresponding effective potentials, examining the existence and stability of circular orbits and the behavior of their characteristic radii. We further investigate the tidal forces experienced by observers in radial… ▽ More In this work, we investigate the geodesic dynamics and tidal force effects in neutral and electrically charged regular spacetimes inspired by T-duality. We first analyze the geometric properties of these solutions, including their regular central structure and horizon configurations, and show how the interplay between the T-duality scale $\ell_0$ and the electric charge $Q$ determines the transition between black hole and horizonless geometries. We then study the motion of massive and massless particles through the corresponding effective potentials, examining the existence and stability of circular orbits and the behavior of their characteristic radii. We further investigate the tidal forces experienced by observers in radial free fall, focusing on the behavior of the radial and angular components, their extrema and sign changes, and the location of these characteristic points relative to the horizon structure of the geometry. We show that the T-duality regularization renders the tidal forces finite in the central region and gives rise to different stretching and compression regimes, including a region of simultaneous compression in both the radial and angular directions, while the electric charge modifies the strength and location of these tidal features. Finally, we analyze the evolution of the geodesic deviation vector for two sets of initial conditions. We find that both the T-duality scale and the electric charge tend to suppress the accumulated radial stretching, whereas the angular deformation is considerably less sensitive to the charge. These results show how the quantum regularization induced by T-duality and the presence of electric charge modify the orbital dynamics and tidal response of these geometries in the strong-field regime. Furthermore, we demonstrate that the corresponding classical limits are recovered appropriately. △ Less

The concept is realized in granular activated carbon, where rever… ▽ More Metamaterials expand the range of accessible macroscopic responses through architectural design. Here, a cross-scale design strategy is introduced that combines microscopic control of static constitutive properties with spectral engineering through macroscopic architecture. Beyond shaping frequency-dependent responses, this strategy modifies the corresponding broadband performance bounds in passive systems by engineering the static parameters entering causal constraints. The concept is realized in granular activated carbon, where reversible sorption enhances equilibrium compressibility. Extrapolation of a multiscale model supported by low-frequency measurements to the zero-frequency limit yields an effective static bulk modulus approximately half the conventional nonsorbing isothermal reference value. This reduction substantially lowers the corresponding causal minimum-thickness bound for rigidly backed absorbers. A cascaded hierarchical composite architecture further redistributes absorption toward the target frequency band, achieving near-perfect absorption with a normal-incidence absorption coefficient above 0.9 across most of the 40--6400 Hz range. Together, static constitutive control and macroscopic spectral design enable ultrabroadband sound absorption beyond the conventional isothermal thickness benchmark without violating causality. This strategy offers a new approach to extending the attainable performance of metamaterials. △ Less

    Authors: Yu Luo, Lina Pu, Yaroslav Koshka, Chun-Hung Liu, Junming Diao

    Abstract: …and event timing. Four commercial SPAD modules were monitored in darkness for more than 920 h. Controlled experiments reproduced temperature-related threshold crossings,… ▽ More Single-photon avalanche diodes (SPADs) are essential components of quantum communication receivers. In practical systems, some SPADs exhibit intrinsic step-like changes in dark-count rate (DCR), commonly referred to as random telegraph signal (RTS) noise. Similar RTS-like variations, however, can also arise from stray light, temperature-dependent threshold crossings, cable disturbances, electrical interference, and other readout artifacts. Distinguishing intrinsic RTS from these artifacts is important because they originate from different mechanisms and require different corrective actions. We present a self-referenced diagnostic that primarily uses the low-to-high afterpulse-yield ratio R, together with complementary checks based on DCR-state reproducibility, cross-channel behavior, and event timing. Four commercial SPAD modules were monitored in darkness for more than 920 h. Three exhibited intrinsic DCR switching of 44.59%, 19.33%, and 2.77%, with corresponding R values of $0.9996\pm0.0027$, $1.0050\pm0.0023$, and $1.0027\pm0.0043$, all consistent with unity. Controlled experiments reproduced temperature-related threshold crossings, light leakage, cable-contact artifacts, and artificial electrical injection. These artifacts produced distinct correlation and timing signatures: temperature-related threshold crossings gave intermediate pair-survival responses, cable disturbances and electrical injection generated sub-dead-time events, while light leakage produced simultaneous cross-channel switching and nonreproducible DCR levels. Combining R with orthogonal timing and DCR-based checks provides a practical self-referenced diagnostic framework for distinguishing intrinsic RTS from non-intrinsic artifacts using primarily time-tagged dark-count data. △ Less

    On the geometric origin of central charges in gravity

    Authors: Lorenzo Di Giacomo, Daniele Pranzetti

    Abstract: …Charge Representation Theorems describing the canonical realization of diffeomorphism symmetries in the presence of field-dependent vector fields and Lagrangian anomalies, corresponding respectively to vanishing and integrable Noetherian flux. In both cases, we derive explicit expressions for the associated central extensions and establish the… ▽ More Central extensions of asymptotic symmetry algebras are traditionally determined through an explicit computation of the algebra of conserved charges. We show that, within an ambiguity-free covariant phase space formalism based on the Noetherian split, they instead arise as intrinsic quantities completely determined by the Lagrangian through the geometry of the associated covariant phase space. We prove two general Charge Representation Theorems describing the canonical realization of diffeomorphism symmetries in the presence of field-dependent vector fields and Lagrangian anomalies, corresponding respectively to vanishing and integrable Noetherian flux. In both cases, we derive explicit expressions for the associated central extensions and establish the corresponding cocycle identities. This provides a constructive algorithm for determining central charges directly from the Lagrangian formulation of the theory, without requiring an explicit computation of the charge algebra. As a non-trivial application, we consider asymptotically locally AdS$_3$ spacetimes with Penrose--Brown--Henneaux boundary conditions. We recover the corresponding centrally extended asymptotic symmetry algebra, providing an explicit realization of the Charge Representation Theorems. △ Less

    Prepare-and-measure quantum key distribution from a bound on information leakage

    Authors: Abhishek Mishra, Michele Masini, Maria Balanzó-Juandó, Armin Tavakoli, Stefano Pironio

    Abstract: …secret bit per key round. For noisy implementations, we compute key rates using semidefinite programming relaxations. Our best protocols tolerate visibilities down to 0.84, corresponding to an 8% quantum bit error rate, compared with the 11% threshold of standard device-dependent BB84. ▽ More Every quantum key distribution (QKD) protocol must limit the information available outside the users' laboratories. In device-independent QKD this is essentially the only assumption made on the devices. In a prepare-and-measure setting it cannot be imposed strictly, since the sender's device must emit a signal that depends on her private input. The natural replacement, and a minimal one, is a leakage bound: a bound on how much information about the input leaves the sender's laboratory, through the intended signal or through any side channel. We ask how far this unavoidable requirement can replace a detailed model of the source. We show that this single bound, quantified by the probability of guessing the input from everything the source emits, suffices to prove security against collective attacks. In our analysis, nothing is assumed about the precise form of the emitted states, their dimension, energy or purity, nor about the receiver's measurement device, beyond the standard requirement that it does not leak Bob's data. The standard B92 and BB84 protocols do not certify any key under this minimal leakage bound assumption. But closely related protocols can and, in the ideal limit, achieve one secret bit per key round. For noisy implementations, we compute key rates using semidefinite programming relaxations. Our best protocols tolerate visibilities down to 0.84, corresponding to an 8% quantum bit error rate, compared with the 11% threshold of standard device-dependent BB84. △ Less

For the weakly singular kernel $t^{α-1}/Γ(α),\; 0<α<1,$ the model captures the transition from parabolic diffusion to wave propagation, recovering the corresponding limiting equations as $α\to0^+$ and $α\to1^-$, respectively, with the memory term governing the intermediate dynamics. The spatial discretization employs time-dependent finite element spaces, while the backward Euler method combined with numerical quadrature is used for time integration. A unified \emph{a posteriori} analysis is established for both semidiscrete and fully discrete approximations. Elliptic reconstruction yields optimal spatial error estimates, while a memory-lag estimate preserves the essential cancellation in the convolution term, yielding a first-order temporal estimator whose kernel dependence is characterized by its integral. The resulting estimators are robust, with constants independent of the final time, spatial mesh size, and time step. Their kernel dependence involves only computable quantities, avoiding exponential growth in time. Moreover, for the weakly singular kernel, the $α$-dependent factors remain uniformly bounded for $α\in(0,1).$ Numerical experiments on a convex domain validate the theoretical results, and a space-time adaptive algorithm is also demonstrated on an L-shaped domain featuring a corner singularity and two temporal layers. △ Less

    Cerebral Artery Wall Displacement and Strain from 4D MRI

    Authors: Anika Paul, Sjoerd B. Vos, Mostafa Jamshidian, Adam Wittek, Karol Miller

    Abstract: …work adapts a registration-based kinematic framework, originally developed for abdominal aortic aneurysms, to cerebral arteries imaged with 7 T 4D phase-contrast MRI. Deformable image… ▽ More Arterial stiffness is an established marker of vascular ageing and cardiovascular risk, yet the local, patient-specific mechanics of the cerebral arteries remain difficult to measure in vivo. Wall deformation over the cardiac cycle is sub-millimetre, rapid and spatially heterogeneous, and is superimposed on larger rigid-body motion. This work adapts a registration-based kinematic framework, originally developed for abdominal aortic aneurysms, to cerebral arteries imaged with 7 T 4D phase-contrast MRI. Deformable image registration between diastole and systole was used to measure wall-normal displacement over the cerebral artery network, primarily the Circle of Willis and connected communicating arteries. Following this, transverse centreline motion was removed to separate local wall deformation from rigid-body vessel motion before estimating circumferential strain. Across 17 datasets, the 99th percentile wall-normal displacement was 0.256 +/- 0.037 mm, 35 % of the acquired voxel dimension, and the 99th percentile circumferential strain was 8.67 +/- 2.40 %. Correction of circumferential strain to exclude rigid translation motion reduced the 99th percentile strain estimation in the example case from 28.1 % to 10.6 %, as a significant portion of the apparent wall displacement corresponds to bulk motion of the vessel. The pipeline produces continuous displacement and strain maps of the intracranial arterial network, although the values are sensitive to segmentation accuracy, resampling and registration parameterisation and require future ground-truth verification. △ Less

    Preserving Separation Logic Specifications through Compilation

    Authors: Carmine Abate, Kleio Liotati, František Farka, Sebastian Ertel

    Abstract: …separation logic carry over to the compiled program. Our proof technique consists of a resource-aware simulation over interaction trees: it relates the resources required by corresponding source and target events, and tracks ownership through target operations, such as register accesses, that have no source counterpart. We apply the approach to a compiler fr… ▽ More Program specifications in separation logic describe ownership of resources and serve as logical interfaces for local reasoning. Compiler correctness, expressed as semantics preservation, does not by itself supply such an interface for compiled code, because it does not explicitly relate source and target resources and assertions. We investigate WP-preserving compilation: when a compiler translates a source program, the weakest preconditions that the program satisfies in a separation logic carry over to the compiled program. Our proof technique consists of a resource-aware simulation over interaction trees: it relates the resources required by corresponding source and target events, and tracks ownership through target operations, such as register accesses, that have no source counterpart. We apply the approach to a compiler from a structured imperative language to an assembly-like language, first for closed programs and then extended with modules and function calls. In the extended setting, each module can be verified in the source logic and compiled separately; its specifications carry over to the compiled module and are preserved when compiled modules are linked. All results are mechanized in Rocq. △ Less

For sufficiently weak gravitational coupling, the branch connectivity changes: the fundamental branches of types I and II reconnect to form one continuous family, while their corresponding second branches form another. We find that, in contrast to the vacuum-connected type I family, the scalaron-supported type II black holes extend to substantially larger horizon radii while preserving a nontrivial scalar configuration outside the horizon. △ Less

The reinterpretation considers the production of… ▽ More A reinterpretation of a prior narrow-resonance search is performed to investigate the resonant production of pairs of dijet resonances via broad mediators. This analysis targets events with four resolved jets, requiring dijet invariant masses greater than 0.2 TeV and four-jet invariant masses greater than 1.6 TeV. The search uses a data sample corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$ collected by the CMS experiment in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV. The reinterpretation considers the production of new heavy four-jet resonances, with widths ranging from 1.5 to 10% of their mass, which decay to a pair of dijet resonances. This analysis probes resonant production in the four-jet and dijet mass distributions. Upper limits at 95% confidence level and significances are reported on the production cross section of new resonances as functions of their widths and masses, between 2 and 10 TeV. In particular, at a four-jet resonance mass of 8.6 TeV, the local (global) significance ranges from 3.9 (1.6) to 3.6 (1.4) standard deviations (s.d.) as the resonance width is increased from 1.5 to 10%. This relative insensitivity to the choice of width indicates that a broad resonance is an equally valid interpretation of this excess. The broad resonance hypothesis at a resonance mass of 8.6 TeV is supported by the presence of an event with a four-jet mass of 5.8 TeV and an average dijet mass of 2.0 TeV. Also, we report the reinterpretation of a second effect, at a four-jet resonance mass of 3.6 TeV, which has a local (global) significance of up to 3.9 (2.2) s.d. △ Less

We introduce Retrieve to Act (R2A), a test-time scaling framework that formulates OOD robustness purely as a geometric motion primitive retrieval problem. R2A bypasses fragile visual features by structuring successful training demonstrations into an offline spatiotemporal motion primitive graph. During inference, a query-dependent Graph Neural Network dynamically localizes the robot's ongoing execution within this graph, retrieving structurally compatible future motion primitives. These retrieved geometric priors are then seamlessly harmonized with the frozen VLA's predicted actions via a learned, dimension-wise gating mechanism, adaptively rectifying erroneous spatial trajectories while preserving the policy's local reactivity. Extensive experiments across three state-of-the-art VLA architectures, GR00T N1.7, $π_{0.5}$, and StableVLA, demonstrate that R2A consistently and significantly enhances robustness against diverse contextual perturbations. R2A achieves absolute success rate improvements of up to +12% in simulation benchmarks (LIBERO-Plus, RoboTwin2.0-Plus) and +40% in real-world deployments, without requiring any finetuning of the base policies. Project page: https://retrieve2act.github.io/r2a/ △ Less

    Authors: Qi-Quan Li, Yu Zhang, Hoernisa Iminniyaz

These results show that the 8-shaped/C-shaped phase structure characteristic of regular AdS black holes persists under rotation, indicating… ▽ More We use the modified Newman--Janis algorithm to obtain the rotating ``mother'' black hole solution of the rotating Hayward-AdS black hole. We further derive the fundamental thermodynamic quantities of the ``mother'' black hole in the full unconstrained phase space $(S,J,q,α,P)$, and impose the regularity condition $M_1=q^3/α$ to obtain the thermodynamic quantities of the rotating Hayward-AdS regular black hole. This order of operations gives a Hawking temperature identical to the surface gravity temperature and produces the correct response functions of the regular black hole. For $P<P_c$, the Hawking temperature $T_2$ possesses a local maximum and a local minimum, which divides the black hole into small, intermediate, and large black hole branches, and the $P$--$V$ isotherms display oscillatory behavior characteristic of a liquid/gas phase transition. The small and large black hole branches are locally stable, whereas the intermediate black hole branch has negative heat capacity and is locally unstable. Increasing the rotation parameter $a$, magnetic charge $q$, or pressure $P$ reduces the region of the intermediate branch. The $G_2$--$T_2$ diagram shows that the phase structure, in contrast to the standard swallowtail of the Reissner--Nordström-AdS and Kerr--Newman-AdS black holes, is 8-shaped or C-shaped. These two shapes are divided by the characteristic pressure $P_z$. For $P<P_z$, the curve is 8-shaped, corresponding to a first-order small/large black hole transition. For $P_z<P<P_c$, the curve is C-shaped, corresponding to a zeroth-order transition. These results show that the 8-shaped/C-shaped phase structure characteristic of regular AdS black holes persists under rotation, indicating that it stems from the restructuring of the thermodynamic phase space by the regularity condition rather than from spherical symmetry. △ Less

Although jointly adapting model parameters and task-specific soft prompts is promising, it has two key limitations: previously learned prompts may lose effectiveness as the model evolves, and relationships among prompts that could support cross-task transfer are not explicitly learned. We propose Learning an Anchored Prompt Space (LAPS), which preserves historical prompt effectiveness and learns relationships among task-specific soft prompts to facilitate positive transfer. Historical prompts are first aligned with the updated model through self-distillation. LAPS then constructs an anchored prompt space whose vertices correspond to learned task-specific soft prompts and whose intermediate geometry is shaped by learnable Bézier control prompts. Once this space is learned, LAPS identifies the best-performing prompt for each task on its validation set, allowing the selected prompt to draw on knowledge acquired from related tasks. Experiments on the TRACE benchmark across three Qwen3 model scales show that LAPS consistently outperforms existing distillation-based, prompt-based, and joint prompt-parameter adaptation methods, improving overall task performance while reducing forgetting. △ Less

We study this behavior in a two-parameter twin experiment and regularize the variational objective. Under Gaussian observation errors and a Gaussian prior, the corresponding minimization objective is the expected Tikhonov calibration objective minus the differential entropy of the approximating distribution. However, the expected Tikhonov term favors concentration near one of the modes, while entropy maximization does not guarantee multimodality. We introduce a regularization term based on Rényi divergence that encourages the approximate posterior to cover the prior support. In the connected bimodal case, a flow approximation represents both modes; in the case with well-separated modes, the flow and Gaussian mixture frequently miss one mode. The introduced regularization reduces the near-collapse fraction from 50% to 25%. While the regularization improves mode coverage in this experiment, it changes the Bayesian variational objective, introduces a trade-off in approximation accuracy, and does not provide a general solution to mode collapse. △ Less

    EEG-Based Decoding of Musical Mode and Melodic Direction Using Empirical Mode Decomposition

    Authors: Sergio Guillén, Lorenzo J. Tardón, Ana M. Barbancho, Isabel Barbancho

    Abstract: …case, accuracies reached 81% on average using the first IMF, with AUC-ROC values of 0.9 on average in both cases. A frequency-domain analysis revealed a strong correspondence between the IMFs and conventional EEG sub-bands, except for certain specific frequency components that were not fully represented. Predominant activations were observed over the frontal… ▽ More Electroencephalography (EEG) has proven to be a powerful tool for decoding neural activity in real-life scenarios, including the perception and processing of emotions elicited by music. Among the various musical features that modulate these responses, musical mode and melodic direction (or contour) play a central role. This work proposes a novel EEG feature extraction framework based on Empirical Mode Decomposition (EMD) to decompose EEG signals recorded during active piano performance for the detection of these two musical features. Energy-based descriptors were computed from the first four Intrinsic Mode Functions (IMFs) obtained through EMD and used to train a Wide Neural Network classifier. The model performance was benchmarked against several standard classifiers commonly employed in EEG classification tasks. Two experimental scenarios were explored: intra-subject and intersubject. In the former case, the proposed framework achieved average accuracies of 84%, whereas in the inter-subject case, accuracies reached 81% on average using the first IMF, with AUC-ROC values of 0.9 on average in both cases. A frequency-domain analysis revealed a strong correspondence between the IMFs and conventional EEG sub-bands, except for certain specific frequency components that were not fully represented. Predominant activations were observed over the frontal, temporal, and parietooccipital regions, areas consistently associated with music processing. Furthermore, the proposed method demonstrated robustness to handedness, successfully classifying EEG data across participants regardless of their dominant hand. These findings highlight the potential of EMD-based feature extraction for identifying music-related neural patterns and contribute to advancing research in Music Information Retrieval (MIR), affective neuroscience, and the broader understanding of the neural encoding of musical structure. △ Less

    Variational Optimal Control for Diffusion Guidance

    Authors: Ruoyu Wang, Xinyi Ye, Yunshuo Shi, Liangyu Yuan, Beier Zhu, Chi Zhang

    Abstract: …and from 7.58 to 4.44 at 25 steps. After 20 updates on Z-Image-Turbo, DVOC improves HPSv3++ and four held-out evaluator scores over the untrained model, with the highest corresponding means among the trained methods compared. These results connect inference-time control and reward adaptation through a common geometric principle. ▽ More We present a variational control framework that connects sampling-time guidance and reward-based model adaptation through a shared Jacobian pullback. Starting from terminal reward maximization with a path-space KL penalty, the Gibbs variational principle and Doob transform yield a correction to the generative dynamics. A local denoising approximation exposes the pullback that transports output preferences to the controlled variable. This construction gives Variational Optimal Guidance (VOG) in state space and extends to Distilled Variational Optimal Control (DVOC) by amortizing locally improved endpoints into model parameters. The resulting controls share a local variational rule: VOG acts through the denoising Jacobian, while DVOC realizes the parameter pullback by endpoint distillation. Under a linear MMSE model, VOG's exact correction lies in the data subspace. On ImageNet, VOG reduces DiT-XL/2 FID from 2.27 to 2.04 at 250 sampling steps and from 7.58 to 4.44 at 25 steps. After 20 updates on Z-Image-Turbo, DVOC improves HPSv3++ and four held-out evaluator scores over the untrained model, with the highest corresponding means among the trained methods compared. These results connect inference-time control and reward adaptation through a common geometric principle. △ Less

    Multidimensional Infinite-Horizon Markovian Backward Stochastic Differential Equations with Continuous Coefficients and Nonzero-Sum Differential Games

    Authors: Said Hamadène, Hongyu Shi, Zhen Wu

    Abstract: …player controls to be fully coupled and permitting payoff criteria that are not restricted to an exponential discount form, establish an equivalence between the game and the corresponding multidimensional infinite-horizon backward stochastic differential equations, and prove the existence of a Nash equilibrium. ▽ More This paper addresses the solvability of multidimensional infinite-horizon Markovian backward stochastic differential equations with continuous coefficients. We do not require the Lipschitz property or other quantitative controls of differences, and each component of the generator is allowed to depend simultaneously on all components of the multidimensional backward variables. A local L^q-domination framework adapted to the infinite-horizon setting is developed and combined with our linear flow conjugacy analysis to obtain the required distributional control in the presence of unbounded linear drift. We also derive a common Markov representation valid for the entire family of initial states. The analytical obstacles arising from weak regularity and the multidimensional structure are thereby overcome. Furthermore, we consider a class of infinite-horizon nonzero-sum differential games with a general non linear structure, allowing player controls to be fully coupled and permitting payoff criteria that are not restricted to an exponential discount form, establish an equivalence between the game and the corresponding multidimensional infinite-horizon backward stochastic differential equations, and prove the existence of a Nash equilibrium. △ Less

    Science-Fiction Transformation as a Design Lens for Visualization Transformation

    Authors: Abdulhaq Adetunji Salako, Christian Tominski

    Abstract: …why is a transformation into a target form needed, or what is the purpose? Second, what remains recognizable across the transformation from source to target, or what is the correspondence? Through four hypothetical transitions between visual representations of a multifaceted dataset, we demonstrate that purpose is also essential to visualization transformati… ▽ More Science fiction often features characters or objects that undergo radical changes in form while still being recognizable as the same entity. For example, a vehicle might unfold into a robot, or one body might flow or shape-shift into another. We use such transformations as a reflective lens to study similar shape-shifting in visualization, where one visual representation transforms into a completely different one. For example, a node-link diagram might transform into a parallel coordinates plot as a user's task changes from understanding relationships to identifying correlations. Based on four instances of science-fiction transformation, our paper highlights two key questions: First, why is a transformation into a target form needed, or what is the purpose? Second, what remains recognizable across the transformation from source to target, or what is the correspondence? Through four hypothetical transitions between visual representations of a multifaceted dataset, we demonstrate that purpose is also essential to visualization transformations. Furthermore, we show that the question of correspondence results in different levels of complexity in terms of designing and understanding transitions. Depending on whether correspondence is 1:1, 1:N, or N:1, visualization transformations may be direct and easy to understand, or they may require more elaborate transition constructs, implying increased cognitive effort for interpretation. Particularly, cases of 1:N and N:1 correspondence pose challenges for designing visualization transformations. △ Less
