    cs.CC quant-ph

    Matrix Samplers and Quantum-Proof Extractors: Their Correspondence and Almost-Optimal Constructions

    Authors: Kuan Cheng, Ruiyang Wu

    Abstract: We establish an equivalence between matrix samplers and quantum-proof randomness extractors. The classical sampler--extractor equivalence is established by a seminal work of Zuckerman (Random Structures & Algorithms, 1997). Using this equivalence, we construct explicit strong matrix samplers with optimal randomness overhead and near-optimal sample complexity via constructing quantum-proof extrac… ▽ More We establish an equivalence between matrix samplers and quantum-proof randomness extractors. The classical sampler--extractor equivalence is established by a seminal work of Zuckerman (Random Structures & Algorithms, 1997). Using this equivalence, we construct explicit strong matrix samplers with optimal randomness overhead and near-optimal sample complexity via constructing quantum-proof extractors. Specifically for matrix dimension $D$, accuracy $\varepsilon$ and failure probability $δ\leq\varepsilon$, our matrix sampler uses $m+O_α(\log(D/δ))$ random bits and $O_α(\varepsilon^{-2-α}\log^{1+α}(D/δ))$ samples, for any constant $α>0$. Compared with the matrix sampler of Xun and Zuckerman (CCC 2025), our construction has improvements in two aspects. Under the same optimal random seed length, we can replace the $\operatorname{poly} D$ dependence in the number of samples by $O(\log^{1-γ} D)$. Under the same scale of sample size, we can remove their $\log\log D$ factor in the length of the random seed. Our underlying strong quantum-proof extractor has output length $n-O_α(a+\log(1/η))$ and seed length at most $(1+α)\log a+(2+α)\log(1/η)+O_α(1)$ for $n$-bit sources with conditional min-entropy $n-a$ and error $η$. We also obtain strong quantum-proof extractors for every constant entropy rate, for any error as small as $2^{-Ω(n)}$, having optimal seed length $O(\log(n/η))$ and output length $Ω(n)$, improving previous constructions. △ Less

    hep-th

    Wilson Towers as Local Bulk Fields

    Authors: Vishal Gayari, Chethan Krishnan

    Abstract: …in 2+1-dimensional gravity can reproduce one-loop partition functions of local free fields in the bulk. Bulk free fields have a second-quantized Fock space, and the AdS/CFT correspondence suggests that the associated multi-particle sectors are dual to multi-trace primaries in the CFT. In this note, we use standard symmetric-function methods to show that the… ▽ More Multi-winding Wilson loops (Wilson spools) in 2+1-dimensional gravity can reproduce one-loop partition functions of local free fields in the bulk. Bulk free fields have a second-quantized Fock space, and the AdS/CFT correspondence suggests that the associated multi-particle sectors are dual to multi-trace primaries in the CFT. In this note, we use standard symmetric-function methods to show that the Wilson spool in thermal AdS$_3$ can be recast as a sum over $single$-winding Wilson loops -- one for each multi-trace primary. In a companion paper to appear, we will view Wilson networks and TQFTs as the natural language of non-perturbative bulk quantum gravity. The present note illustrates how this can apply to $local$ bulk fields, and not just defects: a bulk (generalized free) field is to be viewed as a full tower of multi-trace Wilson lines. We further show that the $SL(2)$ descendants of each multi-trace primary, together with the boundary gravitons of the AdS$_3$ background, correctly reproduce the full Virasoro character of each module. In this language, the role of a light insertion on a heavy primary is played by a topological Verlinde line. This allows us to obtain a "microscopic" description of one-loop determinants on the smooth BTZ handlebody. A spatially wound probe Wilson line on a torus with a contractible $thermal$ cycle can be traded for a Verlinde line inserted on a dense family of heavy Polyakov loops -- with the roles of the two cycles exchanged, so that the $spatial$ cycle is now contractible. The vacuum row of the modular $S$-kernel acts as the (approximate) density of the heavy primaries. This reinforces the case made in arXiv:2601.18775 that a smooth horizon is a stand-in for an ensemble of quantum states, each produced by a heavy Wilson line that appears as a singular horizon in the semi-classical limit. △ Less

    cs.CV cs.AI

    A First-Step Bias Restores Lost Diversity in Diffusion Model Distillation

    Authors: Wenwu Tang, Dong Wang, Yun Cheng, Olga Saukh

    Abstract: …The largest measured reduction in the response to different initial noises occurs during the first, high-noise step. When we replace the first step with the teacher's corresponding trajectory and leave the final three student steps unchanged, most of the output diversity returns. This suggests that the first step is a key point for restoring diversity.… ▽ More Distribution-matching distillation enables high-quality image generation in only a few denoising steps. However, distilled student models often map different initial noise samples to similar outputs under the same prompt. To locate where diversity is lost, we compare the teacher and student along the sampling trajectory. The largest measured reduction in the response to different initial noises occurs during the first, high-noise step. When we replace the first step with the teacher's corresponding trajectory and leave the final three student steps unchanged, most of the output diversity returns. This suggests that the first step is a key point for restoring diversity. Motivated by this finding, we introduce \emph{ReSeed}, a post-distillation method that adds a seed-response bias adapter only to the first student step and aligns its velocity with the teacher's first-interval secant velocity. All student weights and later denoising steps remain unchanged. We evaluate ReSeed on three text-to-image models (SD3-Medium, SD3.5-Medium, and FLUX.1-dev) and one text-to-video model (Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B). For a distilled 4-step FLUX.1-dev model, \emph{ReSeed} requires only $0.00196\%$ additional trainable parameters relative to the base model and is trained on 8,000 prompt--noise pairs, while keeping the distilled student fully frozen and requiring no target images, perceptual losses, or adversarial losses. It improves DINO diversity by $48.3\%$ while maintaining competitive visual quality. These results show that substantial diversity can be recovered through a lightweight, data-efficient tuning of only the first denoising step, without finetuning the full student model. △ Less

    Large-bulk-viscosity limit for two-dimensional compressible micropolar fluids with vacuum

    Authors: Zijian Du, Sen Yang, Xinglong You

    Abstract: …: every sequence of the corresponding solutions admits a subsequence converging to a solution of the two-dimensional inhomogeneous incompressible micropolar system. ▽ More We consider the Cauchy problem for the two-dimensional compressible micropolar fluid system on the torus $\mathbb T^2$. The initial density is bounded and nonnegative and may contain vacuum, while the initial velocity and microrotation belong to $H^1(\mathbb T^2)$. For sufficiently large bulk viscosity $ν$, we prove the global-in-time existence of solutions without a smallness assumption on the $H^1$ size of the data, provided that the initial compressible component is well prepared in the sense that $\sqrtν\|\operatorname{div}u_0\|_{L^2}$ is uniformly bounded. The proof combines the effective viscous flux with a coupled energy scheme that exploits the vorticity--microrotation dissipation at both the basic and material-derivative levels. For the linear pressure law, we also prove uniqueness in the stated solution class by means of a coupled backward parabolic problem, logarithmic interpolation, and Osgood's lemma. For fixed initial data with $\operatorname{div}u_0=0$, we also justify the limit $ν\to\infty$: every sequence of the corresponding solutions admits a subsequence converging to a solution of the two-dimensional inhomogeneous incompressible micropolar system. △ Less

We investigate GUI grounding as a scalable source of spatial supervision for learning observation behaviors that transfer beyond interfaces. We introduce LBS (Look Beyond Screens), a vision-language model trained through supervised initialization on teacher-generated GUI grounding trajectories, followed by reinforcement learning for multi-turn crop-and-inspect decisions. Further analysis suggests complementary roles for the two stages: SFT establishes visual inspection primitives, while RL improves their downstream effectiveness. These findings support GUI grounding as a practical supervision paradigm for transferable active visual perception and broader multimodal reasoning. Our code and model are publicly available at https://github.com/inclusionAI/UI-Venus/tree/LBS and https://huggingface.co/inclusionAI/LBS-27B, respectively. △ Less

    DIP-CAD: A Design-Intent-Preserving Agent Harness for Parametric CAD Modeling

    Authors: Xi Cheng, Chenxi Zhai, Hang Cheng, Mingyu Fan, Pingfa Feng, Long Zeng

    Abstract: …references. On GERD-EVL and a DeepCAD test subset, DIP-CAD improves mIoU for static model generation by 11.1% and 5.6%, respectively. In parameter-editing evaluations, the corresponding mIoU improvements are 7.0% and 4.2%. Case studies further demonstrate how parameter edits drive shape updates while preserving design intent. ▽ More Parametric CAD modeling requires not only matching the target shape, but also preserving design intent under parameter edits. However, most existing methods focus on improving static shape generation accuracy, while the dependencies that govern later edits are often overlooked. To tackle this issue, we present DIP-CAD, a design-intent-preserving agent harness for generating parametric CAD models from images. DIP-CAD combines vision-language model reasoning with CAD kernel operations to establish dependencies that encode the design intent. In parametric CAD, these dependencies are mainly established through geometry references in modeling commands and constraints in parametric sketches. At the command level, ID-based explicit geometry reference binds modeling commands to selected faces, edges, or vertices. At the sketch level, a local-coordinate bridge ensures accurate sketch placement, providing the basis for correctly establishing sketch constraints. Constraints among sketch primitives and constraints to external projected geometry further preserve both internal sketch relationships and dependencies on existing geometry. To provide a more comprehensive data foundation for parametric CAD modeling tasks, we introduce the Geometry Explicit Reference Dataset (GERD), whose structured data format links stepwise modeling commands to intermediate CAD models through explicit geometry references. On GERD-EVL and a DeepCAD test subset, DIP-CAD improves mIoU for static model generation by 11.1% and 5.6%, respectively. In parameter-editing evaluations, the corresponding mIoU improvements are 7.0% and 4.2%. Case studies further demonstrate how parameter edits drive shape updates while preserving design intent. △ Less

    On the Vertices of Delta-modular Polyhedra

    Authors: Bludov Mikhail, Gribanov Dmitry, Klimenko Maxim, Kupavskii Andrey, Lángi Zsolt, Rogozin Alexander, Voronov Vsevolod

    Abstract: …corresponding to a triangulation of $P$'s normal fan. Assuming that $A$ is integer, such polyhedra are called $Δ$-modular polyhedra. Note that in the integer case, the bound can be simplified via the inequality $Δ_{\text{average}} \geq Δ_{\min} \geq 1$, where $Δ_{\min}$ is the minimum absolute value of subdeterminants of $A$… ▽ More Let $P$ be a polytope defined by the system $A x \leq b$, where $A \in R^{m \times n}$, $b \in R^m$, and $\text{rank}(A) = n$. We give a short geometric proof of the following tight upper bound on the number of vertices of $P$: $$ n! \cdot \fracΔ{Δ_{\text{average}}} \cdot \text{vol}(B_2) \sim \frac{1}{\sqrt{πn}} \cdot \left(\frac{2 π}{e}\right)^{n/2} \cdot n^{n/2} \cdot \fracΔ{Δ_{\text{average}}}, $$ where $Δ$ is the maximum absolute value of $n \times n$ subdeterminants of $A$, and $Δ_{\text{average}}$ is the average absolute value of subdeterminants of $A$ corresponding to a triangulation of $P$'s normal fan. Assuming that $A$ is integer, such polyhedra are called $Δ$-modular polyhedra. Note that in the integer case, the bound can be simplified via the inequality $Δ_{\text{average}} \geq Δ_{\min} \geq 1$, where $Δ_{\min}$ is the minimum absolute value of subdeterminants of $A$ corresponding to feasible bases of $A x \leq b$. For this, we prove and use a symmetric variant of Macbeath's theorem. Additionally, we give a direct argument based on prior results in the field, showing that the graph diameter of $P$ is bounded by $O\bigl(n^3 \cdot \fracΔ{Δ_{\min}} \cdot \ln (n \fracΔ{Δ_{\min}}) \bigr)$. Thus, both characteristic of $P$ are linear in $Δ/Δ_{\min}$. From an algorithmic perspective, we demonstrate that: Given $A \in Q^{m \times n}$, $b \in Q^m$, and an initial feasible solution to $A x \leq b$, the convex hull of $P$ can be constructed in $O(n)^{n/2} \cdot m^2 \cdot \fracΔ{Δ_{\text{average}}}$ operations. For simple polyhedra, the dependence on $m$ reduces to linear; Given $A \in Z^{m \times n}$ and $b \in Q^m$, the number $|P \cap Z^n|$ can be computed in $O(n)^n \cdot \frac{Δ^4}{Δ_{\text{average}}}$ arithmetic operations. △ Less

    Hierarchical Low-Rank Reparameterization for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning

    Authors: Rong Huang, Yi Xiong, Yuan-Yuan Cheng, Xiao-Ming Fu

    Abstract: …parameter count of the original methods is compressed by approximately an order of magnitude, our method can still maintain competitive task performance and even surpass the corresponding baselines in average accuracy across the eight commonsense reasoning benchmarks. ▽ More Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods adapt large models by introducing a small number of trainable parameters. However, we find that these parameters still contain redundancy and can be further compressed while maintaining strong task performance. To this end, we propose a novel hierarchical low-rank reparameterization method. Its core idea is to reorganize the trainable parameters of PEFT methods into an approximately square matrix and then express it via a low-rank decomposition, which can be applied recursively. This forms a multi-level structure with factors compressed level by level, drastically reducing the number of trainable parameters. In our experiments, we combine this reparameterization with LoRA, DoRA, MoSA, and RaSA. We evaluate commonsense reasoning on LLaMA-3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-7B, with additional GSM8K math reasoning experiments on LLaMA-3.1-8B. Under comparable trainable-parameter budgets, our method combined with LoRA or DoRA achieves better performance.     Scattering amplitudes from geometry of kernel-free functionals on piecewise random null paths with memory

    Authors: Hiren Kakkad, Piotr Kotko

    Abstract: …that together with the Parke-Taylor denominator build scattering amplitudes. Lorentz invariance fixes the spins of the inserted fields and the homogeneities of the corresponding twistor functions without any use of supersymmetry. The three-point outputs reproduce the kinematic forms of the light-cone cubic vertices of chiral higher-spin theory, and the deriv… ▽ More We introduce a simple functional of algebra-valued fields with definite Lorentz properties that is kernel-free yet it encodes the structure of tree-level scattering amplitudes. The fields are evaluated at the points of a piece-wise straight paths in the anti-self-dual plane of complexified Minkowski space, with the directions of consecutive segments correlated so that the path has a velocity memory. We show that momentum-space reduction of the functional generates the cyclic Parke--Taylor factor, off shell in terms of light-front bilinears and on shell in terms of spinor brackets. The same reduction, organized differently, can be naturally promoted to twistor space without any twistor-space input: a closed cyclic chain of Penrose transforms on a twistor line, in which the Parke--Taylor denominators arise as boundary terms of the integrals along the paths. Finally we show that derivatives with respect to one of the path parameter generate numerators that together with the Parke-Taylor denominator build scattering amplitudes. Lorentz invariance fixes the spins of the inserted fields and the homogeneities of the corresponding twistor functions without any use of supersymmetry. The three-point outputs reproduce the kinematic forms of the light-cone cubic vertices of chiral higher-spin theory, and the derivative monomials of order four generate the components of the $\overline{\rm MHV}$ supermultiplet of $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory, off shell and on shell. △ Less

    Higher-order strongly intensive observables for forward-backward multiplicity correlations

    Authors: Evgeny Andronov

    Abstract: …TeV are used to illustrate the corresponding model-dependent scenarios. ▽ More Forward-backward multiplicity correlations probe the longitudinal structure of particle production, but fluctuations in the number of emitting sources complicate their interpretation. Strongly intensive observables remove the contribution of source-number fluctuations at second order. Here we extend the construction to all cumulant orders. For independent identical sources whose emission law does not depend on the source count, we build a normalized hierarchy of strongly intensive observables with $n$ new algebraically independent quantities at order $n$. Every observable is normalized to unity for statistically independent particles, vanishes when both window multiplicities are fixed, and remains finite at equal positive window means. We give explicit expressions through fourth order and an all-order recursion. Analytical particle production models and PYTHIA calculations for $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=0.9$ and 13 TeV demonstrate the sensitivity to window geometry, event selection, and connected multiparticle correlations. Fits constrained by the mean multiplicities and second-order observables provide conditional higher-order benchmarks. Deviations of these benchmarks from the PYTHIA results test the assumed correlation hierarchy, subject to the quality of the lower-order fit. Preliminary ALICE measurements at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV are used to illustrate the corresponding model-dependent scenarios. △ Less

    AI-Assisted Discovery of CNOT Decompositions for Quantum Permutations on Constrained Topologies

    Authors: Daniele Lizzio Bosco, Jacopo Cossio, Simone Faro, Francesco Pio Marino, Gabriele Messina, Carla Piazza, Giuseppe Serra

    Abstract: …gates required by the corresponding SWAP-based construction. The resulting method subsumes optimal SWAP routing within… ▽ More Efficient quantum circuit compilation is challenging for quantum computers, where limited qubit connectivity can introduce substantial overhead in the implementation of logical operations. Recent AI-assisted synthesis methods have shown considerable potential for reducing circuit complexity, but their role in uncovering principles that can guide the design of new deterministic compilation algorithms remains largely unexplored. In this work, we investigate this perspective for the synthesis of quantum permutations into CNOT circuits under constrained qubit connectivity. Using AlphaCNOT as an exploratory tool, we identify a recurring decomposition for remote transpositions that improves on the construction based on adjacent SWAP routing. For two qubits at graph distance $d>1$, the discovered pattern yields a topology-legal implementation using at most $6d-4$ CNOT gates, compared to the $6d-3$ gates required by the corresponding SWAP-based construction. We formalize this observation through a constructive proof to define Global Transpositions, a deterministic synthesis method that searches for a minimum-cost decomposition of an entire permutation into topology-aware logical transpositions. The resulting method subsumes optimal SWAP routing within its synthesis model and provides certified upper bounds on the topology-constrained CNOT cost. Our experiments also reveal circuits that cannot be expressed as concatenations of independently synthesized transpositions. Instead, these circuits exploit intermediate linear transformations that temporarily leave the permutation subgroup and combine partial effects of multiple logical operations, a mechanism we call intermediate CNOT fusion. Our results show that AI-assisted synthesis can support not only circuit optimization, but also the discovery of reusable identities, provable constructions, and hidden mechanisms in quantum circuit compilation. △ Less

The analysis thus establishes a generic local entropy maximum and a finite global result within the stated geometric rigidity class, ide… ▽ More We develop a geometric--analytic proof of the reverse isoperimetric inequality for compact AdS black hole horizons in Einstein gravity. At fixed thermodynamic volume, a York slice/Jacobi variation of the gravitational action shows that the spherical Schwarzschild--AdS solution is a strict local entropy maximum against generic non-spherical deformations. The finite global extension is formulated within the hypotheses of the Sherif--Dunsby (SD) rigidity theorem: compactness and spherical topology, the required intrinsic Ricci decomposition $\mathrm{Ric}_h=α\,\mathbf e\!\otimes\!\mathbf e+βN$ with $α\neqβ$, a proper conformal transformation of the hypersurface metric given by $\widetilde h_{ab}=e^{2\varphi}h_{ab}$ that preserves its intrinsic scalar curvature, $\widetilde R\equiv R[\widetilde h]=R[h]\equiv R>0$, and negative sheet expansion. Compactness and $S^3$ topology are built into the class considered here. In the static spherical sector such as Schwarzschild--AdS and RN--AdS, the SD Ricci form is naturally realized while it is an additional restriction for Kerr--AdS case. For the $S^3$ topology, a positive Yamabe type conformal class exists for which the Yamabe theorem provides a representative of positive and constant scalar-curvature. We further restrict to the scalar-curvature preserving conformal transformation required by the SD theorem. The negative sheet expansion is obtained using gravitational focusing. Within this admissible class, a non-round Kerr--AdS horizon cannot maximize the entropy, while fixed thermodynamic volume selects the corresponding round Schwarzschild--AdS comparator. The analysis thus establishes a generic local entropy maximum and a finite global result within the stated geometric rigidity class, identifying the role of gravity in reversing the ordinary isoperimetric problem. △ Less

    Authors: Vansh Kharbanda, Anton F. Burnet, Benedikt Sabass

    Abstract: …two-dimensional systems, this suppression is monotonic for nonzero coupling modes and guarantees a monotonic decrease of the entropy production rate (EPR), with the corresponding probability currents vanishing in the strong-coupling limit. In higher dimensions, suppressing the circulation associated with the coupled degrees of freedom does not generally elim… ▽ More Interactions shape the properties of many physical and biological nonequilibrium systems, but whether collective operation raises or lowers entropy production remains difficult to predict. Here, we show that strong coupling suppresses irreversible circulation associated with the directly coupled degrees of freedom. In identical two-dimensional systems, this suppression is monotonic for nonzero coupling modes and guarantees a monotonic decrease of the entropy production rate (EPR), with the corresponding probability currents vanishing in the strong-coupling limit. In higher dimensions, suppressing the circulation associated with the coupled degrees of freedom does not generally eliminate all irreversible circulation, since the remaining degrees of freedom can still sustain nonequilibrium currents. The EPR of these residual dynamics sets the strong-coupling limit, providing a criterion for whether a given choice of coupled variables leads to lower dissipation than the uncoupled system. The approach to this limit depends on how the finite-coupling current correction aligns with the residual current. Further, we show that convergence to residual dynamics in the strong-coupling limit also applies to coupled non-identical systems. Finally, we demonstrate the predicted EPR reduction in nonlinear models of vertebrate hair-cell bundles and bacterial chemotaxis. △ Less

The first paper of this series characterized computability through continuous extendibility in a framework for floating-point computation. Limits of continuous computations may nevertheless be discontinuous, as familiar iterative algorithms demonstrate. We identify the regime in which these limits remain of the first Baire class and apply its structure theory to classify their complexity. For countably many real-valued features, and assuming continuous extendibility of computations, the absence of the independence property on the prescribed invariant bounded regions is equivalent to first-Baire-class regularity of all limits of the extended computations on the corresponding completed regions. Every such limit is consequently the pointwise limit of a sequence of extended computations on each completed region. For countable families acting continuously on a compact metrizable state space, this is the tame regime of topological dynamics, whose hierarchy Glasner and Megrelishvili obtained from Todorčević's trichotomy. We transport that classification to computation structures and realize every level by a rotation, the power method, the planar and spatial Bresenham algorithms, and the action generated by Euclidean steps on the projective line. Newton's method for the square roots of unity on the Riemann sphere lies beyond the tame regime. Under the same extendibility assumption, a measure-theoretic counterpart for arbitrary feature families characterizes the absence of independence by featurewise universal measurability of the same limits. For every Radon input probability measure, each output feature is then uniformly approximable by polynomials in the input features outside a set of arbitrarily small probability. △ Less

This means the physical qPAH and observed LPAH/LFIR do not evolve in lockstep. (4) The PAH-metallicity relationship (PZR) arises naturally in this framework: galaxies enrich and grow their diffuse ISM fraction simultaneously, linking rising metallicity to rising qPAH. Our models represent the first to reproduce the PZR observed across z=0-2. (5) The LPAH-SFR and LPAH-M_mol relations emerge from two effects: more massive galaxies have larger PAH reservoirs, and higher-SFR galaxies excite their PAHs more efficiently per unit mass. Taken together, these results suggest that grain-grain shattering in the diffuse ISM is the main driver behind the evolution of cosmic PAH abundances. △ Less

    Abstract Lipschitz Continuity

    Authors: Marco Campion, Isabella Mastroeni, Michele Pasqua, Caterina Urban

    Abstract: …in particular of program semantics, requiring that bounded differences between properties of two inputs yield proportionally bounded differences between properties of the corresponding outputs. ALC explicitly discerns between two complementary forms of approximation: property-based (or qualitative) approximations, which retain a property of the data and are… ▽ More We introduce Abstract Lipschitz Continuity (ALC), an extensional property of functions, in particular of program semantics, requiring that bounded differences between properties of two inputs yield proportionally bounded differences between properties of the corresponding outputs. ALC explicitly discerns between two complementary forms of approximation: property-based (or qualitative) approximations, which retain a property of the data and are formalized by upper closure operators as in abstract interpretation, and quantitative approximations, which measure the closeness of data and are formalized by pre-metrics, a weakening of metrics that covers the distances arising in program analysis. Keeping the two forms of approximation explicit has two main benefits. First, it allows us to precisely relate ALC to established extensional properties: ALC is a sufficient condition for partial completeness in abstract interpretation, for partial abstract non-interference in language-based security and, with a null Lipschitz constant, for abstract robustness in machine learning. Second, it supports reasoning about ALC of program semantics by induction on the syntax of programs: we present a sound deductive system, parametric in the property-based approximations and in the pre-metric of interest, that reduces a proof of ALC for a program to proofs for its basic commands and to some explicit side conditions, which isolate what makes ALC compositional across sequential composition, non-deterministic choice and iteration. △ Less

At the heart of this theory is an intrinsic geometric characteristic, determined entirely by the dihedral angles surrounding a polyhedral face. We establish a unimodular Gauss-Bonnet identity and show that the expectation of this local geometric invariant completely determines the global conformal type: under suitable assumptions, a random ideal polyhedron is almost surely parabolic precisely when the expected characteristic vanishes, and hyperbolic precisely when it is negative. Combining these geometric tools with unimodular probability methods, we identify the Poisson boundary of the face random walk and establish positive linear hyperbolic speed.     Abstract: We present an analysis of QCD non-linear (NL) effects in the coherent photoproduction of charmonia off nuclei at high energies corresponding to future measurements at the LHC.     Extensions of Operator Systems, Hyperrigidity and Quantum Double Suspension

    Authors: Vibhor Bhatt, Satyajit Guin, Palash Sharma

    Abstract: We introduce a class of extensions of unital operator systems satisfying a certain Toeplitz type property and show that these extensions induce corresponding extensions of the $C^*$-algebras they generate. We prove that an extension in this class is hyperrigid in the $C^*$-algebra it generates if and only if both operator systems from which it is constructed… ▽ More We introduce a class of extensions of unital operator systems satisfying a certain Toeplitz type property and show that these extensions induce corresponding extensions of the $C^*$-algebras they generate. We prove that an extension in this class is hyperrigid in the $C^*$-algebra it generates if and only if both operator systems from which it is constructed are hyperrigid in the $C^*$-algebras they generate. We then introduce the quantum double suspension of operator systems as a special case of this construction and show that its iterates yield the Hong-Szymański quantum double suspensions at the $C^*$-algebra level, including the Vaksman-Soibelman quantum spheres. In particular, we construct hyperrigid operator systems that generate the $C^*$-algebras of these quantum spheres. As a further application, we show that every counterexample to Arveson's hyperrigidity conjecture gives rise to a sequence of counterexamples. We identify the Šilov ideal and the $C^*$-envelope of these extensions and derive a $K$-theoretic condition under which each such sequence contains infinitely many pairwise non-isomorphic counterexamples. In particular, Scherer's counterexample gives rise to infinitely many pairwise non-isomorphic counterexamples. Finally, we compute the propagation number, an invariant of operator systems introduced by Connes and van Suijlekom, for this class of extensions. △ Less

    Sharp global \(L^2\) estimates for oscillatory integral operators with quasi-homogeneous phases

    Authors: Minbeom Kang, Joonil Kim

    Abstract: …polynomial phases. We introduce a family of Newton polygons adapted to the regions near and away from the real zero curves of the Hessian, together with the corresponding box-area scales. The relative geometry of these Newton polygons and area scales characterizes global \(L^2\)-boundedness and determines the sharp \(L^2\)-estimates in the high- and low-freq… ▽ More For one-dimensional oscillatory integral operators with real-analytic phases, the seminal work of Phong and Stein established that the Newton polygon of the mixed Hessian of the phase determines the sharp local \(L^2\)-decay. The local theory relies on spatial localization near the origin and therefore does not capture the global geometry of the real zero set of the Hessian. For globally defined operators, unbounded real zero curves of the Hessian can make the global decay strictly slower than the local decay or destroy \(L^2\)-boundedness altogether. We establish a global analogue of the Phong--Stein theory for quasi-homogeneous polynomial phases. We introduce a family of Newton polygons adapted to the regions near and away from the real zero curves of the Hessian, together with the corresponding box-area scales. The relative geometry of these Newton polygons and area scales characterizes global \(L^2\)-boundedness and determines the sharp \(L^2\)-estimates in the high- and low-frequency regimes. As an application, we determine the exact range of anisotropic homogeneous \(L^2\)-Sobolev estimates for the associated global Radon transform. △ Less

We show that (i) the relation expressed as a Fourier-mode constraint on Shulga's compact cycle, (ii) the same relation as a Descartes curvature condition in the sense of Kocik, and (iii) the Soddy completion of the resulting circle configuration are related by exact algebra, with no approximations. In the first part of the paper, we consider the Fourier transform as a bijective mapping on $S_H$, the space of all $H$-valued smooth rapidly decaying functions on $\R^n$, which naturally extends bijectively to the dual space $S_H'$. Within this framework, we characterize the Fourier transform in terms of the convolution theorem, supplemented by additional structural properties of the Fourier transform. In the second part of the paper, we consider the discrete setting, where the Fourier transform is an operator on $L^1(\Z/n\Z, H)$, and we provide corresponding characterization based on its essential properties. In contrast to the classical setting, a key assumption in our framework involves the interplay between two distinct convolution operations. As a consequence, the structure of our proof diverges notably from the classical case and introduces additional complexities. △ Less

Accounting for the full signal chain, our analysis indicates more than an order of magnitude lower latency relative to previous custom electronic and photon… ▽ More Transformers achieve state-of-the-art performance in language processing and computer vision. Their computation increasingly depends on hardware that can keep pace not only with large matrix multiplications, but also with nonlinear operations like Softmax within the core attention mechanism. On digital accelerators, Softmax accounts for only a small fraction of total operations, yet it can disproportionately impact overall inference latency. Here, we use thin-film lithium niobate Mach-Zehnder modulators (MZM) as analog computing elements for low-latency Transformer attention. Our high-speed nonlinearities based on the intrinsic sinusoidal MZM response achieve performance close to digital baselines in Vision Transformers and GPT-2, even under 4-bit input and output quantization. We experimentally characterize the electro-optic response at symbol rates up to 10 GBaud and assess model robustness under corresponding noise conditions. Accounting for the full signal chain, our analysis indicates more than an order of magnitude lower latency relative to previous custom electronic and photonic Softmax implementations. These results establish analog electro-optic devices as nonlinear function units well suited for integration into emerging co-packaged electronic-photonic hardware, offering a path toward high-speed Transformer inference. △ Less

    TDLM: Adaptive Temporal Integration for Diffusion Language Models

    Authors: Bohang Sun, Max Zhu, Francesco Caso, Christopher Irwin, Jialin Yu, Pietro Liò

    Abstract: …faster generation than the corresponding original diffusion language model, with competitive answer quality on the evaluated tasks. These results motivate temporal memory as a practical way to make more effective use of repeated denoising computation. ▽ More Masked diffusion language models repeatedly compute representations for unresolved tokens, but conventional denoisers do not explicitly retain these representations for reuse across steps. We introduce Temporal Self-Conditioning Diffusion Language Model (TDLM), which lets each unresolved position attend to its own history of hidden representations within a frozen denoiser. This gives later predictions access to computation already performed. Training uses self-generated trajectories and a simple cross-entropy objective for tokens revealed between consecutively sampled decoding steps, while causal temporal attention permits parallel evaluation during training. On LLaDA, the temporal modules add only approximately 0.84% trainable parameters. Across LLaDA and Dream, the main comparison shows up to 7.15$\times$ faster generation than the corresponding original diffusion language model, with competitive answer quality on the evaluated tasks. These results motivate temporal memory as a practical way to make more effective use of repeated denoising computation. △ Less

    Authors: Qing Yang, Yuqi Xiong, Pengcheng Huang, Zhipeng Xu, Zhenghao Liu, Zulong Chen, Yu Gu, Ge Yu

    Abstract: …signal accessibility from its use as action evidence. Layer-wise analyses show that exposure-dependent action evidence becomes more pronounced toward the model output, with stronger evidence under Text, whil… ▽ More External skills provide language-model agents with reusable procedural knowledge, yet how models use such knowledge after it is supplied remains poorly understood. We identify Procedural Exposure Bias (PEB), the tendency of language models to increasingly favor an action as its associated procedural pattern is repeatedly exposed in a skill, even when the interaction state and action relevance remain unchanged. We find that PEB strongly depends on how procedural knowledge is represented: presenting the same content visually substantially attenuates exposure-induced shifts in action preference relative to textual presentation. Further analysis shows that this attenuation primarily arises from visual encoding rather than structured layout. Although repeated procedural signals remain recoverable from Visual representations, they exert a weaker influence on downstream action prediction, distinguishing signal accessibility from its use as action evidence. This effect also extends to executable action selection, where increasing exposure more strongly favors the corresponding action under Text than Visual. Layer-wise analyses show that exposure-dependent action evidence becomes more pronounced toward the model output, with stronger evidence under Text, while activation interventions reveal architecture-specific roles of internal components in transmitting or counteracting the preference shift. Complete-agent evaluations across ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, and WebShop further show that Visual skills achieve higher macro-average task success than Text skills for all four evaluated models. These findings establish skill representation as an important factor governing how procedural knowledge is translated into agent decisions. All code is available at https://github.com/NEUIR/Vis-Skill. △ Less

A comparison with representative sensitivity curves of planned space-based gravitational-wave detector… ▽ More We formulate a description of self-gravitating Schrödinger--Poisson (SP) solitons in curved spacetime using a local inertial frame centered on the soliton. In the weak-field and non-relativistic regime, the effect of the background spacetime is incorporated into the SP equations through an effective tidal potential determined by the spacetime curvature. We apply this framework to a soliton moving along a circular equatorial geodesic around a Kerr black hole. The tidal field contains stationary and time-dependent quadrupolar components, which induce a deformation of the soliton and an associated mass quadrupole moment. We formulate the quadrupolar response in terms of the tidal deformability and the dimensionless Love number. We then estimate the gravitational radiation associated with the rotating deformed soliton and evaluate the corresponding characteristic strain for representative soliton sizes and orbital radii. A comparison with representative sensitivity curves of planned space-based gravitational-wave detectors identifies parameter regimes in which the estimated characteristic strain may lie within their sensitivity ranges. Our framework provides a systematic basis for investigating the tidal response of self-gravitating bosonic solitons in black-hole spacetimes and their potential gravitational-wave signatures. △ Less

    Maximum Energy of Integral Circulant Graphs with Same-Parity Exponents

    Authors: Jianwei Jiang, Chunhua Yang

    Abstract: …p and q are distinct primes at least five and a and b, both positive integers, have the same parity. Among all nonempty sets D of proper divisors of n, the energy of the corresponding integral circulant graph is maximised by exactly two divisor sets: the divisors p to the power i times q to the power j with i plus j odd, and the divisors with i plus j even a… ▽ More The energy of a finite graph is the sum of the absolute values of the eigenvalues of its adjacency matrix. Let n be p to the power a times q to the power b, where p and q are distinct primes at least five and a and b, both positive integers, have the same parity. Among all nonempty sets D of proper divisors of n, the energy of the corresponding integral circulant graph is maximised by exactly two divisor sets: the divisors p to the power i times q to the power j with i plus j odd, and the divisors with i plus j even after removing p to the power a times q to the power b. We also determine the exact maximum energy. For the proof, weighted prime-power Ramanujan transforms yield an exact tensor-product representation of the graph energy, reducing the extremal problem to a weighted cut separating two prescribed vertices. After a sign conjugation, the relevant transforms decompose into embedded path blocks; the nontrivial blocks correspond to a weighted path together with chords of length three of weight negative one. We prove a sharp lower bound for cuts separating the two prescribed vertices in Kronecker products of these path blocks. For products of two such nontrivial blocks, equality holds only for cuts that isolate one of the two prescribed vertices. Combined with the energy-to-cut correspondence, these equality cases yield exactly the two maximising divisor sets. △ Less

Panoramic SLAM and incremental SfM rely on feature correspondences, but repeated, similar structures and small partial overlap make reliable long-range connections difficult to establish, leaving accumulated drift unresolved. Feed-forward models offer detailed local geometry, but their high computational costs limit inference window size, necessitating additional constraints for global consistency. This motivates combining continuous feature-based tracking and sparse mapping with rapid, high-quality local reconstruction from feed-forward models to achieve accurate localization and globally consistent dense mapping over long sequences. We therefore propose a panoramic reconstruction framework that couples an ORB-based sparse panoramic mapping frontend with a fine-tuned feed-forward model. Our key insight is that predicted local geometry enables 3D verification of feature matches across distant revisits, yielding long-range camera constraints even between regions. We calibrate predicted depths with sparse landmarks and use verified connections to optimize the global pose graph, updating dense geometry with the corrected camera poses. We also introduce PanoRecon, a seven-scene benchmark with repetitive architecture, large and small loops, and reference camera poses and LiDAR geometry for evaluating global consistency. Our method achieves the lowest mean absolute trajectory error and high F-1 Score among the evaluated methods. Project Page:https://zhuoxiaoli.github.io/HoloRecon_page/ △ Less

To this end, we introduce two classes of hesitant tree automata and… ▽ More We study the expressive power of First-Order Logic (\FO) over (unordered) infinite trees, with the aim of identifying robust characterisations in terms of branching-time specification formalisms. While such correspondences are well understood in the linear-time setting, the branching-time case presents well-known structural challenges. To this end, we introduce two classes of hesitant tree automata and show that they capture precisely the expressive power of two branching-time temporal logics, namely \PolPCTL and \CTLsf, both of which have been previously shown to be equivalent to \FO over infinite trees. These results provide uniform automata-theoretic characterisations and yield a natural normal form for the latter in terms of a new fragment of \CTLs called \PolCTLs. As a consequence, we identify a fundamental limitation of \FO in this setting: along each branch, it can express only properties that are either safety or co-safety, thereby revealing a sharp expressive boundary for first-order definability over infinite trees. △ Less

    UniPCH: A Unified Framework for Cross-Chain Payments via Payment Channel Hubs

    Authors: Yuanming Shao, Yuming Feng, Weizhe Zhang, Bin Xiao, Song Guo, Zejun Wang

    Abstract: …updates, and settle balances. Some protocols do not explain how their channels are implemented on actual blockchains. Supporting a new ledger or execution model requires a corresponding channel realization and may also require protocol adaptation and security analysis. We present UniPCH, a framework with two layers that separates native channel execution fro… ▽ More Payment channel hubs (PCHs) enable private cross-chain payments, but existing protocols remain difficult to extend across heterogeneous blockchains. Their payment flows often depend on how particular blockchains represent transactions, authorize channel updates, and settle balances. Some protocols do not explain how their channels are implemented on actual blockchains. Supporting a new ledger or execution model requires a corresponding channel realization and may also require protocol adaptation and security analysis. We present UniPCH, a framework with two layers that separates native channel execution from private cross-chain payment logic. The lower UniPC layer defines common state transitions and security requirements. It separates task authorization from confirmation of the complete channel state. We provide realizations for UTXO ledgers and account ledgers that support smart contracts. At the upper layer, we develop selective re-signing blind conditional signatures (S-RBCS) to coordinate conditional authorization across hubs. Using S-RBCS, we construct a payment protocol that coordinates different channels in four phases. Our analysis establishes local UniPC security, griefing resistance, economic atomicity, and session unlinkability. We implement a Go prototype and evaluate the running time and bandwidth of each phase under different network conditions and system parameters. Results show modest computational overhead from the common interfaces. Most evaluated configurations run faster than comparable schemes under network delay. A full refresh ablation shows that reusing valid task evidence reduces computation and communication costs in the account realization. △ Less

We further introduce a small perturbation in one of the Kähler moduli that modifies the existing potential in the field space. The effective correction in the moduli space is made by introducing a localised Gaussian bump, which induces an ultra-slow-roll feature in the inflaton potential during the early cosmological inflation. The evolution of the amplitude of the curvature power spectrum is solved numerically by using the Mukhanov-Sasaki equation, with Bunch-Davies initial conditions. The enhancement of curvature power spectra lies in the sensitivity region of the SKA observations, suggesting an indication that scalar-induced gravity waves may be probed in future observations. △ Less

Rate constraints on nonlinear functio… ▽ More Birkhoff pseudospectral methods replace the differentiation matrices of classical transcriptions with integration operators whose conditioning does not deteriorate with polynomial order. This paper presents theoretical and computational advancements toward their practical use in aerospace optimal control. The initial-point-anchored Birkhoff integration operator is constructed explicitly for flipped Legendre-Gauss-Radau grids and related exactly to the corresponding Lagrange differentiation matrix and, through a reflection, to the standard Radau Birkhoff operator, whose conditioning properties it inherits. Rate constraints on nonlinear functions of states and controls are enforced directly through the pseudospectral operators and, for control-rate limits, shown to be primal and dual equivalent to state augmentation. Non-affine time mappings concentrate the nodes around localized trajectory features, and composing affine scaling and equilibration transformations provides a systematic way to construct scaling strategies. The methods are implemented in the open-source MATLAB framework RAPTOR and tested across a range of challenging optimal control problems, including a dense-grid orbit-raising problem, a rate-limited landing scenario, a Space Shuttle entry problem, and a five-phase reusable-launch-vehicle mission. △ Less

    Authors: Luigi M. Malgieri, Filippo Ascolani, Matteo Giordano, Matteo Ruggiero

    Abstract: …laws admit exact representations as mixtures of Dirichlet distributions. For pre… ▽ More We study signal prediction and smoothing in hidden Markov models whose latent signal is a multi-type Wright-Fisher diffusion and whose discrete-time observations are categorical. We consider a repeated-sampling regime with finitely many inspection times and large within-time samples. Under neutrality and parent-independent mutation, the Bayesian predictive and smoothing laws admit exact representations as mixtures of Dirichlet distributions. We show that the one-step predictive and local smoothing laws converge in total variation to the corresponding Wright-Fisher transition and bridge laws determined by the neighboring population frequencies, thereby justifying plug-in approximations based on empirical proportions. For prediction, we derive explicit finite-sample bounds for this approximation and for bounded predictive functionals such as diversity, together with a finite-sample concentration bound for the marginal smoother. The joint posterior at the inspection times concentrates at the target frequencies and satisfies a Bernstein-von Mises limit with distinct interior and boundary regimes, with Gaussian and Gamma limits on different scales. The results allow arbitrary dependence across inspection times. A key technical ingredient is a fixed-interval tail bound for the coalescent-based dual death process. △ Less

To this end, we adopt a th… ▽ More World Action Models (WAMs) learn representations for robot control by coupling future visual prediction with action generation. Successful manipulation depends primarily on how the robot and task-relevant objects evolve, whereas future-video prediction must also reconstruct the appearance and background of the entire scene. We therefore propose Trace-WAM, which augments video-action learning with future 3D motion prediction as an auxiliary objective. This objective supervises temporal point correspondence together with image-plane displacement and depth change, directly describing the interaction dynamics and spatial changes involved in manipulation. To this end, we adopt a three-branch Mixture-of-Transformer (Tri-MoT) architecture to jointly learn video prediction, motion prediction, and action generation. The motion expert predicts future trajectories from current visual features, allowing motion supervision to shape the representations used for action generation. Because the action expert does not consume predicted trajectories, inference retains the current-observation policy path: a single visual forward pass followed by action denoising. Experiments on RoboCasa-GR1, LIBERO, and LIBERO-Plus demonstrate strong in-distribution performance and out-of-distribution generalization, with particularly clear gains over Fast-WAM under limited demonstrations and distribution shifts. △ Less

    Kinetic Sectors and Nonequilibrium Dynamics in the $δ$-Ising Model

    Authors: Dehen Cao, Wei Jiang, Ya Zhang

    Abstract: …. Parameter sweeps correspond to ordered products of sector operators, which separate reaching a state from holding it, so hysteresis appears when a state is reached and released at different sector boundaries. We also show how increasing the system size can induce a Mpemba-like reversal, allowing a state with more misaligned spins to reach the final ordered… ▽ More We introduce and analyze the $δ$-Ising model, a kinetic Ising ferromagnet in which single-spin flips are accepted when their energy change does not exceed a tunable threshold. Varying the field and threshold opens or closes local flip channels, dividing parameter space into kinetic sectors, each governed by a fixed Markov transition operator. We prove that the dynamics retains global spin-inversion symmetry exactly when up and down spins have equal numbers of open channels. In one such sector on a periodic chain of length $L$, unbiased random initial states therefore reach the field-aligned and antifield states with probability $1/2$ each. Closing a single channel leaves both states absorbing but makes the probability of ordering against the field decay as $(2/π)^L$. Parameter sweeps correspond to ordered products of sector operators, which separate reaching a state from holding it, so hysteresis appears when a state is reached and released at different sector boundaries. We also show how increasing the system size can induce a Mpemba-like reversal, allowing a state with more misaligned spins to reach the final ordered state sooner on average. On a periodic chain, this reversal arises because the mean absorption time grows logarithmically with system size for many short domains but linearly for a single macroscopic domain, and it disappears when the bulk flip channel is opened. These findings show how a simple, intuitive rule for Ising dynamics links local kinetic constraints to field response, memory, and relaxation within a unified framework. △ Less

    Weakness Localization Is Not Enough: State-Aware Recursive Knowledge Improvement for Large Language Models

    Authors: Runze Jiang, Yan Wang, Boran Jin, Bingyu Zhu, Longtao Huang

    Abstract: …rounds. At each round, RKI localizes knowledge weaknesses, estimates the model's operational knowledge state from diagnostic evidence and intervention history, selects a corresponding intervention, and re-evaluates the updated model before determining the next intervention. On HotpotEval and SafeInternalEval, RKI outperforms existing knowledge incorporat… ▽ More Knowledge improvement in large language models is shifting from predefined knowledge incorporation pipelines toward evaluation-guided updating. Existing methods use evaluation to localize model-specific knowledge weaknesses and guide targeted post-training. However, identifying what to improve does not necessarily provide sufficient information for deciding how to improve it. The same localized weakness can be compatible with distinct operational knowledge states: the relevant knowledge may be unavailable, available but difficult to access reliably, or accessible but ineffective for downstream use. We call this ambiguity knowledge-state aliasing. When compatible states favor different interventions, greater diagnostic resolution alone is insufficient for intervention selection. Moreover, model updates can change the underlying knowledge state before the observed weakness disappears, such that an intervention appropriate in one round may no longer address the bottleneck in the next. To address these challenges, we propose Recursive Knowledge Improvement (RKI), which formulates knowledge improvement as a sequential, state-aware intervention process. RKI organizes knowledge into graph-structured units that provide a shared basis for diagnostic evaluation and targeted intervention across rounds. At each round, RKI localizes knowledge weaknesses, estimates the model's operational knowledge state from diagnostic evidence and intervention history, selects a corresponding intervention, and re-evaluates the updated model before determining the next intervention. On HotpotEval and SafeInternalEval, RKI outperforms existing knowledge incorporation methods by 18.41 points on average. Further analyses show that knowledge states provide additional guidance for intervention selection beyond weakness localization and can change across model updates even when the observed weakness persists. △ Less

    Holographic pressure and volume for black holes

    Authors: Silvester G. A. Borsboom, Manus R. Visser

    Abstract: …law includes a surface pressure conjugate to the boundary area. Assuming the existence of a holographically dual theory living on this boundary, these geometric quantities correspond to the pressure and volume of the dual thermal system. In this work we focus on static, spherically symmetric black holes, for which these quantities reduce to global thermodyna… ▽ More We advocate for a holographic definition of thermodynamic pressure and volume for black holes based on quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics. When a black hole is enclosed by a finite timelike boundary, York's quasi-local first law includes a surface pressure conjugate to the boundary area. Assuming the existence of a holographically dual theory living on this boundary, these geometric quantities correspond to the pressure and volume of the dual thermal system. In this work we focus on static, spherically symmetric black holes, for which these quantities reduce to global thermodynamic variables. The holographic volume provides a notion of system size, allowing extensivity to be defined in standard thermodynamic terms, and it yields a definition of the large-system limit. For the asymptotically flat case, we show that, in the canonical thermodynamic representation, small Schwarzschild black holes are non-extensive, whereas large black holes become extensive in the large-system limit. A similar conclusion applies to Anti-de-Sitter Schwarzschild black holes, with the difference that the quasi-local energy of the large black hole also becomes extensive in the large-system limit. Before this limit, the energy decomposes into subextensive and extensive contributions, and we derive an explicit expression for the extensive part as a function of the finite volume and entropy. △ Less

However, existing designs remain constrained by a persistent trade-off among anthropomorphic kinematic complexity, mechanical robustness, load capacity and control simplicity, limiting dexterous manipulation in unstructured environments. Here we present Torch-Hand, a fully articulated humanoid dexterous hand that replicates the joint structure and motion hierarchy of the human hand with 21 degrees of freedom (DOF), comprising a highly articulated five-DOF thumb and four DOF in each of the remaining fingers. The differentiated thumb integrates multi-axis CMC motion to provide broad opposition and flexible contact orientation relative to the four fingers. Unlike tendon-driven or hybrid actuation approaches, Torch-Hand adopts an entirely linkage-based transmission architecture. By integrating screw-linkage actuation, the hand achieves high grasping force and substantial payload capacity while maintaining a compact anthropomorphic form. Its actuation architecture is optimized for control tractability, with all actuators integrated within the palm and arranged in a serial-robot-like configuration that establishes a direct correspondence between motor motion and joint kinematics. This architecture mechanically decouples functional DOF to reduce unintended inter-joint motion propagation, while allowing independently addressable DOF to be actively coordinated into coupled multi-joint motion patterns during complex manipulation. Comprehensive experiments assess reachable workspace, coordinated fingertip trajectories, thumb opposition, 33-grasp taxonomy coverage, grasping force, payload capacity and task-level manipulation. These results establish Torch-Hand as a robust hardware foundation for dexterous embodied manipulation. △ Less

This raises a question: how can observations from different views be matched to the correct identities without offline scene-specific training or camera calibration? Progress toward this goal remains limited because many offline formulations assume such deployment-specific knowledge, while online adaptation can amplify association errors. We study this problem as online multi-view identity maintenance (OMIM): targets are initialized once per view, with no further identity labels provided after each initialization. To this end, we introduce OmniGlimpse, an asymmetric propose-verify framework that separates adaptive association from stable identity consolidation. At the representation level, short-term local and cross-view evidence is mapped into target-specific adaptive spaces to propose associations, whereas long-term identity distributions are maintained for verification in a canonical space defined by a frozen encoder. At the update level, short-term evidence may guide current associations, whereas, after bootstrapping, only verified observations may update persistent memory or drive adaptation. Under a unified target-specified evaluation protocol, OmniGlimpse improves over the best evaluated online baseline for each metric by 9.1 IDF1 and 22.1 CVIDF1 points on M3Track. The corresponding gains on MvMHAT are 20.3 and 26.2 points.     Authors: Hansol Hong, Siu-Cheong Lau, Ju Tan

We obtain local charts and gluing maps of the noncommutative mirror varieties via explicit computations of Lagrangian deformation spaces and Fukaya isomorphisms between them. These constructions recover noncommutative spaces studied by Kapustin-Kuznetsov-Orlov, Baranovsky-Ginzburg-Kuznetsov, and Kawamata, with further examples in dimension three. We also establish the categorical framework in the unobstructed setting. For a compact $\mathbb Z$-graded unobstructed immersed Lagrangian $\mathbb{L}$ without nonpositive-degree immersed generators, Koszul duality identifies the derived Fukaya subcategory generated by $\mathbb{L}$ with the derived category of modules with finite-dimensional total cohomology over its extended localized mirror $\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{L}}$. This construction starts from the Floer algebra of the compact reference brane and does not require a global wrapped generator. Applications to plumbings recover known split-generation results for compact objects. △ Less

We characterize the Green's functions of all strictly positive real self-adjoint realizations of this operator. The kernels are in one-to-one correspondence with positive-semidefinite $2\times2$ endpoint covariance matrices. We call the associated centered Gaussian processes Brownian processes. Each is a standard Brownian bridge plus the affine interpolation of an independent Gaussian endpoint vector. Singular endpoint covariance matrices give hard endpoint constraints, such as the pinned start $X(0)=0$ of Brownian motion. This parametrization gives explicit operator domains and energy spaces. On every mesh it gives an exact sparse precision matrix, so the likelihood of $n$ observations can be evaluated in $O(n)$ operations. Nonnegative realizations with a null space are treated separately through orthogonally normalized pseudoinverses, which are proper Gaussian covariances. △ Less

While these multimodal features are largely shared across individuals, they remain an underutilized source of information in analyzing neural population dynamics. We posit that these shared biological profiles provide a common reference for relating neural dynamics across subjects and recording sessions. Together, these results suggest that organizing neural populations by their multimodal biological features offers a promising framework for generalizing population dynamics across subjects and recording sessions. △ Less

    Authors: Emil Mottola

    Abstract: …corresponds to a cohomologically trivial cocycle, it also arises from the IR limit of massless quantum fields, independently of UV physics, and contributes to the WZ action for low energy gravity. Analogous to the WZ action for pseudo-Goldstone mesons in low energy QCD from the axial anomaly, the WZ action for the conformal anomaly provides a Nambu-Goldston… ▽ More The complete Wess-Zumino (WZ) action and stress-energy tensor (SET) of the conformal anomaly in both two and four dimensions is given. This local effective action is expressed in terms of a single scalar Nambu-Goldstone `conformalon' field $\varphi$, parametrizing the local abelian Weyl group of conformal transformations that act transitively on the spacetime metric. Satisfying WZ consistency, and equipped with a variational principle for $\varphi$ that determines the conformal transformation $g_{μν}\!\to\!g_{μν}e^{-\varphi}$ to an associated spacetime with no anomaly, the WZ action resolves the conformal gauge fixing ambiguity of earlier methods of `integrating the anomaly.' In $D\!=\!2$ the WZ action is the local form of the Polyakov action, whose massless $\varphi$ propagator describes a correlated two-particle scalar intermediate state in the SET polarization function $\langle TT\rangle$. The imaginary part of this polarization obeys a UV finite sum rule, becoming a Dirac $δ$-function at zero energy for conformal quantum fields, demonstrating the infrared origin of the anomaly, while the real part becomes the light cone propagator of $\varphi$. Although the $\Box R$ term in $D\!=\!4$ corresponds to a cohomologically trivial cocycle, it also arises from the IR limit of massless quantum fields, independently of UV physics, and contributes to the WZ action for low energy gravity. Analogous to the WZ action for pseudo-Goldstone mesons in low energy QCD from the axial anomaly, the WZ action for the conformal anomaly provides a Nambu-Goldstone scalar field for macroscopic gravity, in addition to the metric of classical general relativity. △ Less

The top-degree image consists precisely of second cosyzygies in that category and is extension-closed, but is not abelian when $\mathbf t\ne\mathbf0$. For the non-top image categories, we determine attached primes from projective covers and compute the Bass numbers at $\mathfrak m$ and the injective dimensions over $S$ of projective and injective objects. In every non-top degree, we construct an exact functorial section selecting, for each nonzero target, a source of minimal projective dimension $n-i$ and maximal depth $i$ among all its sources. If $n\ge2$ and $\mathbf t\ge\mathbf1$, no fully faithful section exists in degrees $1$ and $n-1$, even without requiring exactness. In top degree, graded Matlis duality followed by $S$-duality gives a fully faithful functorial section. We also describe the global images obtained by varying the determining bound. The non-top global categories have enough injectives and no nonzero projective objects, while the top category corresponds to second cosyzygies in the category of finite-support representations of $\mathbb N^n$. △ Less

    SAGE: Semantic-Augmented Geometric Experts for Point Cloud Registration

    Authors: Tomoyasu Shimada, Kazuhiko Murasaki, Shoichiro Takeda, Shogo Sato, Riku Inoue, Toshihiko Nishimura, Ryuichi Tanida

    Abstract: Point… ▽ More Point cloud registration is an essential task for scene reconstruction, requiring the alignment of partially overlapping point clouds. Under low-overlap conditions, this task remains challenging because existing geometry-only methods cannot distinguish geometrically similar but semantically distinct structures (e.g., TV corners vs. desk corners and chair legs vs. table legs), resulting in incorrect correspondences. To address this geometric ambiguity, we present Semantic-Augmented Geometric Experts (SAGE), which integrates frozen pre-trained point cloud semantic representations into the registration pipeline without requiring additional sensors or fine-tuning the semantic encoder. To effectively leverage these semantic distinctions, SAGE employs a Semantic-Guided Mixture of Experts (SG-MoE) architecture. Through semantic-aware routing, SG-MoE enables each expert to specialize in distinct semantic-geometric patterns, reducing mismatches that confound geometry-only methods. With a structure-aware estimator, SAGE achieves the highest registration recall on 3DLoMatch while remaining competitive on 3DMatch. When evaluated with the same number of refinement iterations, SAGE further achieves the highest inlier ratio and registration recall on both benchmarks. These results indicate that frozen semantic representations provide effective cues for resolving geometric ambiguity in low-overlap registration. △ Less

The teacher and alignment branch are removed after training, enabling privacy-preserving radar-only inference without additional sensing or computational branches. △ Less

We present a reliability-aware framework connecting polarity--time localization, geometric recovery, event--CAD refinement, and trajectory optimization. The simulation evaluates 453,174 decision stuck-at and selected parameter bit-flip events on a 7,400-instance. Primary comparisons use 128 completed configurations. These completed-subset results are distinguished from the simulation summaries covering 140 configurations with enumerable fault sites. Fault outcomes are classified by their security consequences, including evasion, false-alarm increases, and metric silence. A fault is critical when malware recall decreases by at least five percentage points. Tree-based models exhibit a lower mean critical-fault fraction than function-based models, 5.3% versus 14.3%; exhaustive-only analysis preserves this ordering. Moreover, 72.3% of evaluated tree faults are metric-silent, changing both recall and false-positive rate by less than 0.1 percentage points without necessarily preserving individual predictions. Among the 27 completed matched comparisons, Bagging reduces criticality in 26, whereas AdaBoost increases it in two. Finally, an exact minimax selection method identifies protection candidates under additive unit costs and ideal correction. For a representative J48 detector, protecting 16 of 115 structural units reduces predicted maximum recall loss from 60.1 to 3.3 percentage points. These results characterize structural-model sensitivity and potential protection benefits; faulted RTL correspondence and physical hardening overhead remain unvalidated. △ Less

The APS studies the interaction between structures of interest in Probability Theory such as Gibbs distributions, equations with noise, etc. and the axioms of the corresponding objects and transformations. We also introduce in Section 6 Transformations-Axiomatic Based Learning (TABLe) on Mathematical data-sets. One feature of this approach is the ranking of transformations from a table for a certain task based on scores which reflect which transformations work better than others, similarly to when human investigators try various methods on a task. We also study structure recovery from noisy data. In particular, to exemplify the ideas, we consider loss functions which contain derivations and derivations-like transformations. As a first application we study recovery in the context of polynomials with integer coefficients using standard derivatives along with other types of derivations which appear in Arithmetic: the p-derivations which satisfy \textit{different axioms} from the usual derivatives. As a first application of the APS we study a class of noisy (partial/mixed) derivations equations.     Optimizing Evolution Under Cyclic Selection: the Case of B Cells

    Authors: Xingjian Hou, Colin Scheibner, Ned S. Wingreen

    Abstract: …selection events affects adaptation, we develop a minimal computational model of cyclic selection. We find that the optimal average mutation rate per division and the corresponding adaptation velocity are nearly invariant across number of divisions between selections, and we quantitatively explain this behavior with a theory based on the dynamics of the "… ▽ More Rapid evolutionary adaptation may be necessary for organisms facing strong environmental pressures. Although selection often acts continuously, in the case of B cells evolving high affinity antibodies in germinal centers, selection can take place after multiple rounds of division. To study how the number of divisions between selection events affects adaptation, we develop a minimal computational model of cyclic selection. We find that the optimal average mutation rate per division and the corresponding adaptation velocity are nearly invariant across number of divisions between selections, and we quantitatively explain this behavior with a theory based on the dynamics of the "nose", i.e. the fittest individuals in the population. We also find that when the number of divisions between selection events is large, then mutating every division results in slower adaptation than mutating only at the last division before selection (as is the case for B cells). Our model provides a framework for understanding adaptation in natural and experimental systems subject to cyclic selection. △ Less

    Authors: Sumedh R. Risbud

    Abstract: …is the instantaneous power dissipation. This framework establishes that the affine parameter along the trajectory corresponds strictly to the cumulative energy dissipated. While one may think (owing to the Helmholtz Minimum Dissipation Theorem) that the hydrodynamic resistance tensor could be the natural candidate for a Riemannian metric shaping the particle trajectories, we demonstrate that the trajectories driven by constant external forces are \emph{not} geodesics of this bare resistance metric. Instead, they experience a geometric drift due to the curvature induced by the presence of obstacles. By rigorously incorporating translational-rotational coupling through a modified effective resistance tensor $\tilde{\mathbf{R}} = \mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{FU}} - \mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{Fω}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{Tω}}^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{Fω}}^T$, which accounts for the hidden dissipation savings afforded by particle rotation, we introduce a unified geometric formalism. We prove that the physical particle trajectories are exact geodesics of a conformally scaled metric, $\tilde{g}_{ij} = \mathcal{D}(\mathbf{x})\tilde{R}_{ij}$, where $\mathcal{D}$ is the instantaneous power dissipation. This framework establishes that the affine parameter along the trajectory corresponds strictly to the cumulative energy dissipated. We apply this theory to the scattering of a spherical particle by a fixed spherical obstacle, showing that the exact analytical trajectory derived previously by the author is recovered as a geodesic of the dissipation-scaled manifold. △ Less

In this paper, we introduce LEVIRDet-159, the largest and most comprehensive remote sensing object detection dataset to date, with 159 categories, 2.56 million bounding boxes, and 700k fine-grained annotations under a multi-level taxonomy. In each key scale dimension, LEVIRDet-159 exceeds the corresponding largest existing remote sensing object detection dataset, containing approximately (7x) more images, (6x) more object instances, and (4x) more categories. Based on this dataset, we design LEVIRDetNet, a scale-hierarchy-aware detection foundation model for universal remote sensing object detection. LEVIRDetNet couples online visual Ground Sampling Distance (GSD) prediction, GSD-conditioned query modulation and allocation, and a hierarchy-aware detection head for mixed-granularity remote sensing supervision. Under stringent evaluation settings, LEVIRDetNet demonstrates strong cross-domain generalization. Even without target-domain training or fine-tuning, it achieves state-of-the-art detection performance on 9 external benchmarks, improving the strongest fully supervised competing methods by 5.02 mAP on average under each benchmark's primary metric. We hope this study will facilitate the development of strongly generalizable remote sensing object detection across diverse category systems, spatial resolutions, and sensor platforms. The full image tiles, annotations, source-license manifest, code, and trained models have been released in a versioned project repository at https://qinzheyang.github.io/LEVIRDet/. △ Less

We report the serendipitous discovery of a giant ORC, ORC~J1928$-$5056, in 856~MHz continuum data from the ASKAP observations. The emission forms a single, mildly elliptical structure $4^{\prime}$ across, corresponding to $0.63$~Mpc at the redshift $z=0.1507$ of the central galaxy LEDA~140850, the brightest cluster galaxy of Abell~3639, which makes it one of the largest single ORCs known. After subtracting the radio nucleus associated with the host galaxy, the ORC has a flux density of 73~mJy integrated within an area of $\sim 12$~arcmin$^2$, i.e.\ a mean surface brightness of only $1.7$~$μ$Jy~arcsec$^{-2}$, and a rest-frame luminosity of $L_{1.4}=2\times10^{24}$~W~Hz$^{-1}$, comparable to those of the most luminous ORCs. Including its nucleus, the system reaches $L_{1.4}=6.6\times10^{24}$~W~Hz$^{-1}$, higher than that of any other ORC reported so far. The host is a massive ($M_{\ast}\sim10^{11}\,M_{\odot}$), passive elliptical galaxy with negligible star formation. It contains an AGN jet with a power-law spectrum of index $α=-0.877\pm0.089$ between 856 and 1656~MHz. Finally, we discuss the implications for ORC formation models and the observations needed to establish the nature of this system. △ Less

Much existing theory of feature learning has focused on the emergence of a global low-dimensional representation. We show that this picture is incomplete. In regression problems with clustered data, we demonstrate that multilayer perceptrons (MLPs) develop specialized neurons: individual neurons become strongly aligned with specific features relevant to a particular region of the input space. Thus, rather than learning a single global low-dimensional representation, MLPs learn a collection of local low-dimensional representations. Furthermore, we show that this ability to specialize gives MLPs a provable data-efficiency advantage over feature-learning methods based on a global low-dimensional representation. △ Less
